**וַיִּשָּׂתְרוּ.** כְּמוֹ וַיִּסָּתְרוּ, שֶׁהֲרֵי הוּא בַּמָּסוֹרֶת כְּתוּבִים שִׁי״ן, וּמְפֹרָשִׁים בִּלְשׁוֹן סָמֶ״ךְ, מַכַּת בֵּית הַסְּתָרִים:


**And they were afflicted internally.**וַיִשָֹּתְרוּ is identical with וַיִסָּתְרוּ for it is listed in the Masora among those words written with a 'שֹ' and explained as though it were written with a 'ס' meaning "there was a plague in the hidden parts of the body."²

footnotes:
² Or it may mean that the plague occurred at night when it was dark and they were unable to see the שְׁרָצִים, i.e., the plague was hidden from them.—Da’as Sofrim



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Samuel 5:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/5:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/5:11:1)


**מְהוּמַת מָוֶת.** כָּל מְהוּמוֹת עַל יְדֵי רַעַם, וְזֶה אֲבִיהֶן, ״וַיַּרְעֵם ה׳ בְּקוֹל גָּדוֹל (בַּיּוֹם הַהוּא) עַל פְּלִשְׁתִּים וַיְהֻמֵּם״ (שמואל א ז:י), כָּךְ מְפֹרָשׁ בְּאַגָּדַת שְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁתַּיִם מִדּוֹת דְּרַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר בְּנוֹ שֶׁל רַבִּי יוֹסֵי הַגְּלִילִי:


**Deadly upheaval.** All upheavals [mentioned in Scripture] are caused by thunder. The following is the origin: "Adonoy thundered with a great noise on that day against the Philistines, making them panic.³ It is so explained in the Aggadah of Thirty-Two Rules of R. Eliezer, the son of R. Yose Haglili.

footnotes:
³ Below 7:10.



## Chapter 6


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:2:1)


**בַּמֶּה נְשַׁלְּחֶנּוּ.** בְּאֵיזֶה עִנְיָן נְשַׁלְּחֶנּוּ שֶׁלֹּא יֶחֱרֶה אַפּוֹ, וְיָשׁוּב חֲרוֹנוֹ מִמֶּנּוּ:


**How we should send it back.** In what manner should we send it away, so that He will not be angry, and so that His wrath will be removed from us.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:3:1)


**אָשָׁם.** הֶכֵּר דָּבָר שֶׁאַתֶּם מוֹדִים שֶׁמְּעַלְתֶּם בּוֹ:


**A guilt-offering.** As an indication that you confess that you have betrayed Him.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:3:2)


**אָז.** תֵּרָפְאוּ וְנוֹדַע לָכֶם שֶׁהוּא עָשָׂה, כִּי לָמָּה לֹא תָסוּר יָדוֹ אָז מִכֶּם:


**Then you will be healed, and you will realize.** That He did this, for why wouldn't His hand turn away from you then?



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:4:1)


**סַרְנֵי פְלִשְׁתִּים.** חֲמִשָּׁה הֵם, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (יהושע יג:ג): ״הָעַזָּתִי וְהָאַשְׁדּוֹדִי הָאֶשְׁקְלוֹנִי הַגִּתִּי וְהָעֶקְרוֹנִי״:


**Philistine rulers.** They were five, as it is stated, "The Azites, the Ashdodites, the Ashkelonites, the Gittites, and the Ekronites."¹

footnotes:
¹ Yehoshua 13:3.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:7:1)


**עָלוֹת.** מְנִיקוֹת:


**Nursing cows.** Nursing.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:7:2)


**אֲשֶׁר לֹא עָלָה עֲלֵיהֶם עֹל.** כָּל זֶה לְנִסָּיוֹן, שֶׁאֵין אֵלּוּ רְאוּיוֹת לִמְשׁוֹךְ, וְעוֹד שֶׁגּוֹעוֹת אַחַר בְּנֵיהֶם, וְאִם יְהֵא כֹּחַ בָּאָרוֹן שֶׁיּוֹלִיכוּ אוֹתוֹ מֵאֲלֵיהֶם, נֵדַע שֶׁהוּא עָשָׂה לָנוּ:


**Which have not borne a yoke.** All this is for the test. Since these cows are not capable of pulling [a load], and furthermore, they will yearn for their young. If the Ark will have the power to enable them to pull it by themselves, we shall know that He brought this upon us.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:8:1)


**בָאַרְגָּז.** שקריניי״ו בלע״ז:


**In the box.** *Escrin* in O. F.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:10:1)


**כָּלוּ בַבָּיִת.** לְשׁוֹן כֶּלֶא:


**Confined in the house.** 'כָּלוּ' is an expression of 'כֶּלֶא' [imprisonment].



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:12:1)


**וַיִּשַּׁרְנָה.** הֲרֵי תֵּבָה זוֹ אַנְדְּרוֹגִינוֹס, מְלַמֵּד שֶׁאַף הַוְּלָדוֹת הָיוּ אוֹמְרִים שִׁירָה: רֹנִּי רֹנִּי הַשִּׁטָּה הִתְנוֹפְפִי וכו׳, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּמַסֶּכֶת עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה (כד ב). וּלְפִי פְשׁוּטוֹ, ׳וַיִּשַּׁרְנָה׳, לְשׁוֹן ׳יְשַׁר דֶּרֶךְ׳, יָשָׁר הָיוּ הוֹלְכוֹת:


**Set on a straight course.** This word is a grammatical hermaphrodite.² This teaches us that even the young recited a song,³ "Sing aloud, sing aloud, O Ark of acacia wood! Exalt yourself…" as stated in Maseches Avodah Zorah.⁴ According to its simple meaning, 'וַיִשַּׁרְנָה' is an expression meaning 'straight,' i.e. they followed a straight path.

footnotes:
² The “י” of וַיִשַּׁרְנָה is the masculine form and the “נה” is indicative of the feminine form.
³ And, therefore, the combination of the masculine and feminine forms.
⁴ 24b. The cows literally sang an intelligible praise to ‘ה. They were given the miraculous power to sing just as Bilam’s donkey was given the power of speech in Bamidbar 22:28-30.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:12:2)


**הָלֹךְ וְגָעוֹ.** לְשׁוֹן צַעֲקַת בָּקָר, גְּעִיָּה:


**Lowing as they walked.** This is an expression of the cry [גְּעִיָה] of cattle.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:12:3)


**בֵּית שֶׁמֶשׁ.** יִשְׂרָאֵל הָיוּ שָׁם:


**Beis Shemesh.** Yisroel was there.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Although בֵּית שֶׁמֶשׁ was one of the cities belonging to kohanim [Yehosua 21:16], nonetheless man Bnei Yisroel lived there and in adjacent areas.—Da’as Sofrim



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:13:1)


**וַיִּשְׂמְחוּ לִרְאוֹת.** הָיוּ מִסְתַּכְּלִין הֵיאַךְ הוּא בָּא לְבַדּוֹ, וּמִשִּׂמְחָתָם נָהֲגוּ בּוֹ קַלּוּת רֹאשׁ, שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ מִסְתַּכְּלִין בּוֹ בְּאֵימָה וְדֶרֶךְ כָּבוֹד:


**And they rejoiced to see.** They were going to see how it came alone, and out of their joy, they behaved with levity, for they did not gaze at it with awe and respect.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ They violated the commandment “and they shall not come in to see when the holy is being covered and [they will not] die,” in Bamidbar 4:20.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:18:1)


**כְּפַר הַפְּרָזִי.** כְּפָר שֶׁאֵין לוֹ חוֹמָה:


**Open village.** A village without a wall.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:18:2)


**אָבֵל הַגְּדוֹלָה.** הִיא הָאֶבֶן הַגְּדוֹלָה הָאֲמוּרָה לְמַעְלָה, שֶׁהַלָּמֶ״ד בָּאָה בִּמְקוֹם נוּ״ן, וְכֵן ׳נִשְׁכָּה׳ בִּמְקוֹם ׳לִשְׁכָּה׳, בְּסֵפֶר נְחֶמְיָה (יג:ז), וּמִפְּנֵי שֶׁאֵרַע לָהֶם אֲבֵלוּת בַּדָּבָר, קְרָאָהּ אָבֵל:


**The large rock.** This is the large rock previously mentioned, for the 'ל' comes in place of the 'נ' [in the word 'אָבֵל'], similar to 'נִשְׁכָּה' instead of 'לִשְׁכָּה' [chamber], in the book of Nechemyah.⁷ And because they experienced mourning there,⁸ they called it 'אָבֵל'.

footnotes:
⁷ 13:7.
⁸ See below verse 19.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Samuel 6:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/6:19:1)


**שִׁבְעִים אִישׁ חֲמִשִּׁים אֶלֶף אִישׁ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וּקְטַל בְּסָבֵי עַמָּא שִׁבְעִין גַּבְרָא, וּבְקַהֲלָא חַמְשִׁין אַלְפֵי גַּבְרָא״. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (סוטה לה ב): שִׁבְעִים אִישׁ, שֶׁכָּל אֶחָד הָיָה שָׁקוּל כַּחֲמִשִּׁים אֶלֶף, אוֹ חֲמִשִּׁים אֶלֶף אִישׁ, שֶׁכָּל אֶחָד שָׁקוּל כְּשִׁבְעִים סַנְהֶדְרִין:


**Seventy men, fifty thousand men.⁹** Targum Yonoson renders, 'And He killed of the elders of the people seventy men, and of the community fifty thousand men.'⁹ Our Rabbis, however said, 'Seventy men, each of whom was equal to fifty thousand, or fifty thousand men each of whom was equal to the seventy of Sanhedrin.'¹⁰

footnotes:
⁹ Another opinion is that this figure also includes the number of Philistines that were killed as a result of their irreverence towards the Ark.
¹⁰ Maseches Sotah 35b.



## Chapter 7


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:1:1)


**קִדְּשׁוּ.** זִמְּנוּ:


**They appointed.** Designated.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:2:1)


**מִיּוֹם שֶׁבֶת הָאָרוֹן בְּקִרְיַת יְעָרִים.** וְרָאוּ הַפּוּרְעָנוּת שֶׁבָּא עֲלֵיהֶם בַּעֲוֹן בֵּית עֵלִי, וְאֶת הַגְּבוּרָה שֶׁעָשָׂה הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בַּפְּלִשְׁתִּים:


**From the time the Ark came to rest in Kiryas Yearim.** And they saw the retribution which befell them because of the sin of Eili's family, and the mighty deed which the Holy One, Blessed is He, had visited upon the Philistines.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:2:2)


**וַיִּנָּהוּ וְגוֹ׳ אַחֲרֵי ה׳.** עַל יְדֵי שְׁמוּאֵל, שֶׁהָיָה מְחַזֵּר מֵעִיר לְעִיר, וְשׁוֹפְטָם וּמוֹכִיחָם:


**The house … was drawn after Adonoy.** Through Shmuel who would travel from city to city, judging and reproving them.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:2:3)


**וַיִּנָּהוּ.** לְשׁוֹן הַמְשָׁכָה הוּא, וּלְשׁוֹן אֲרָמִי הוּא: תִּתְּנֵהוּ לְפוּלְחָנִי, וְכֵן ״וְלֹא נֹהַּ בָּהֶם״ (יחזקאל ז:יא), אֵין בָּהֶם תּוֹעֶלֶת לְמָשְׁכָם אֶל הַטּוֹב. וּמְנַחֵם חִבְּרוֹ ״וַיִּנָּהוּ״ לְשׁוֹן יְלָלָה, כְּמוֹ ״נְהִי נִהְיָה״ (מיכה ב:ד), וְכֵן ״וְלֹא נֹהַּ בָּהֶם״ (יחזקאל ז:יא), וּפֵירוּשׁ ״וַיִּנָּהוּ אַחֲרֵי ה׳״, שֶׁהִתְאַבְּלוּ עַל מַעֲשֵׂיהֶם הָרָעִים וְשָׁבוּ אַחֲרֵי ה׳. לָשׁוֹן אַחֵר: ״וַיִּנָּהוּ״ כְּמוֹ ״וְיִתְאַסְּפוּ וְנִקְווּ אֵלֶיהָ כָּל הַגּוֹיִם״ (ירמיהו ג:יז), מְתַרְגְּמִינָן: ״וְיִתְנַהוֹן לְמִפְלַח בָּהּ כָּל עַמְמַיָּא״, וְיִהְיֶה מִזֶּה הָעִנְיָן גַּם כֵּן ״וְלֹא נֹהַּ בָּהֶם״ (יחזקאל ז:יא), כְּעִנְיָן שֶׁאָמַר ״לֹא מֵהֶם וְלֹא מֵהֲמוֹנָם וְלֹא מִנֹּהַּ שֶׁבָּהֶם״, כְּלוֹמַר וְלֹא מִקִּבּוּץ שֶׁלָּהֶם (ספרים אחרים אינו):


וַיִנָּהוּ' is an expression of drawing; in Aramaic, 'תִּתְּנֵהוּ לְפוּלְחָנִי' [they were drawn after My worship]. And similarly 'וְלֹא נֹהַּ בָּהֶם' it is no use to attract them to good.¹ Menachem classifies 'וַיִנָּהוּ' as an expression of lamentation, as in 'נְהִי נִהְיָה' [a lament has come to be].² And similarly 'וְלֹא נֹהַּ בָּהֶם' [is rendered as, 'and they will have no remorse']. And the meaning of 'וַיִנָּהוּ אַחֲרֵי ה' is, "they lamented their evil deeds, and returned after 'ה." Another explanation is that 'וַיִנָּהוּ' means 'and they gathered,' as in, "And all the nations will be gathered into it [Yerusholayim]"³ which the Targum renders, "And all nations will gather into it to worship." In this manner, the verse 'וְלֹא נֹהַּ בָּהֶם' may be rendered לֹא מֵהֶם "neither from them," וְלֹא מֵהֲמוֹנָם "nor from their multitude וְלֹא [מִ]נֹהַּ [שֶׁ]בָּהֶם meaning nor from their gathering.

footnotes:
¹ Yechezkeil 7:11.
² Michoh 2:4.
³ Yirmiyohu 3:17.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:6:1)


**וַיִּשְׁאֲבוּ מַיִם וַיִּשְׁפְּכוּ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וּשְׁפִיכוּ לִבְּהוֹן בִּתְיוּבְתָּא כְּמַיָּא קֳדָם ה׳״. וּלְפִי מַשְׁמָעוֹ, אֵינוֹ אֶלָּא סִימַן הַכְנָעָה, הֲרֵי אָנוּ לְפָנֶיךָ כְּמַיִם הַלָּלוּ הַנִּשְׁפָּכִין:


**They drew water and poured it out.⁴** Targum Yonoson renders, "and they poured out their hearts like water, in repentance, before '."⁵ה And according to its simple meaning, it is merely symbolic of humility, [i. e.,] we are before You like these poured out waters.⁶

footnotes:
⁴ Perhaps as Dovid did in II Shmuel 23:16.
⁵ See Eichah 2:19.
⁶ Water may also be referring to the Torah which is often compared to water. By filling their hearts with Torah, the Bnei Yisroel were able to recognize their sins, feel the need to change their ways and return to ‘ה.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:6:2)


**וַיִּשְׁפֹּט שְׁמוּאֵל.** בֵּין אִישׁ לְרֵעֵהוּ, עַל עִסְקֵי מָמוֹן שֶׁבֵּינֵיהֶם, אוֹ עַל עִסְקֵי עֲבֵירָה שֶׁעָבְרוּ:


**Shmuel judged.** Between two litigants regarding monetary matters between them,⁷ or concerning sins which they had committed.

footnotes:
⁷ People sought to cleanse themselves of sins that had been committed between their fellow man. They first had to rectify their misdeeds. They therefore went to Shmuel, for him to judge them and to determine who owed what to whom and what wrongs had to be corrected.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:9:1)


**וַיַּעֲלֶה עוֹלָה.** וַיַּעֲלֶהָ כְּתִיב, נְקֵבָה הָיְתָה, מִכָּאן לָמְדוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ: עוֹלָה נְקֵבָה כְּשֵׁרָה בְּבָמַת יָחִיד, בְּמַסֶּכֶת עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה (כד ב):


**And offered it all as a burnt offering.** The כְּתִיב is 'וַיַעֲלֶהָ' because it was a female.⁸ From here our Rabbis deduced that a female animal is acceptable as a burnt offering on a private altar.⁹

footnotes:
⁸ Although the קְרִי is in the male form.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:11:1)


**מִתַּחַת לְבֵית כָּר.** ״מִלְּרַע לְבֵית שָׁרוֹן״:


**Until below Beis Kor.** Below Beis Sharon.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 7:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/7:12:1)


**וּבֵין הַשֵּׁן.** שֵׁן הַסֶּלַע:


**And Shein.** The peak of the rock.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Or שֵׁן may denote the shape of the rock, i.e. it was shaped like a tooth; and it may be the same crag referred to below in 14:5.—Da’as Sofrim.



## Chapter 8


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 8:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:3:1)


**וַיִּטּוּ.** הֵם עַצְמָם:


**They were swayed.** Themselves.¹

footnotes:
¹ וַיִטּוּ is in the קַל [simple] form.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 8:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:3:2)


**וַיַּטּוּ.** דָּבָר אַחֵר, דְּהַיְינוּ הַמִּשְׁפָּט. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (שבת נו א): לֹא חָטְאוּ בְּנֵי שְׁמוּאֵל, אֶלָּא לֹא הָלְכוּ בְּדַרְכֵי אֲבִיהֶן, שֶׁהָיָה מְחַזֵּר בְּכָל מְקוֹמוֹת יִשְׂרָאֵל וְשׁוֹפְטָן בְּעָרֵיהֶן, וְהֵם לֹא עָשׂוּ כֵן, כְּדֵי לְהַרְבּוֹת שָׂכָר לְחַזָּנֵיהֶם וּלְסוֹפְרֵיהֶם:


**[They] perverted.** Something else, i.e., justice.² Our Rabbis said³ that Shmuel's sons did not sin. They merely did not follow in their father's ways. Their father would travel to all places in Yisroel, and judge them in their cities. But they did not do so,⁴ in order to increase the income of their agents and scribes.

footnotes:
² וַיַטּוּ is in the הִפֽעִיל [causative] form.
³ Maseches Shabbos 56a.
⁴ They did not travel, but rather settled in בְּאֵר שֶׁבַע and would summon litigants to their court. Because the litigants had to pay additional travel expenses to go to בְּאֵר שֶׁבַע, many chose to forgo their claims and thereby corrupting the justice system.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 8:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:6:1)


**וַיֵּרַע הַדָּבָר.** לְפִי שֶׁאָמְרוּ לְשָׁפְטֵנוּ כְּכָל הַגּוֹיִם:


**The matter was wrong.** Because they said, "to judge us like all the nations."⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Shmuel was not displeased at the Bnei Yisroel’s request, because the Torah explicitly permits the institution of the monarchy in Devarim 17:15. It therefore seems that seeking to appoint a new king, now that the nation had settled in Eretz Yisroel was appropriate. What disturbed Shmuel was that the motive behind the request was not a desire to fulfill a mitzvah of appointing a king. Rather, they were complaining and rejecting Shmuel’s leadership [below verse 8] and their reason for seeking to appoint a king was because of their desire to be “…like all the nations…”



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 8:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:9:1)


**הָעֵד תָּעִיד בָּהֶם.** הַתְרֵה בָּהֶם, שֶׁתְּהֵא אֵימַת מַלְכָּם עֲלֵיהֶם:


**But warn them.⁶** Warn them that the fear of their king be upon them.⁷

footnotes:
⁶ See Bereishis 43:3.
⁷ In order to impress Bnei Yisroel with awe for the monarchy, i.e., the king’s position, Shmuel was instructed to teach them the honor due to a king of Yisroel.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 8:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:12:1)


**וְלַעֲשׂוֹת כְּלֵי מִלְחַמְתּוֹ.** לַעֲשׂוֹת אוֹתָם אֻמָּנִים וְנַפָּחִים וְנַגָּרִים:


**And to produce his implements of war.** To train them as craftsmen, blacksmiths, and carpenters.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 8:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/8:13:1)


**לְרַקָּחוֹת.** מְתַקְּנוֹת מִרְקָחִים וּבְשָׂמִים, לְתַמְרוּקֵי נָשָׁיו:


**For perfumers.** Who prepare perfumes and spices for his wives' cosmetics.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Being that this verse deals with the preparation of food, רַקָּחוֹת may also be translated as confectioners or as people who produce the various spices used in the preparation of meats.



## Chapter 9


### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:1)


**בְּאֶרֶץ צוּף.** ״בְּאַרְעָא דְּבָהּ נְבִיָא״:


**To the land of Tzuph.** [Targum renders] 'into the land wherein there was a prophet.'


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:2)


**צוּף.** כְּמוֹ ׳צוֹפֶה׳:


**Tzuph.** [צוּף] meaning a seer.¹

footnotes:
¹ See above 1:1.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:3)


**פֶּן יֶחְדַּל אָבִי מִן הָאֲתֹנוֹת.** שֶׁתִּגְדַּל דְּאָגָה שֶׁלָּנוּ עָלָיו, עַד שֶׁיִּשְׁכַּח אֶת הָאֲתוֹנוֹת:


**Lest my father stop worrying about the donkeys.** Because his worry about us will grow so that he will forget the donkeys.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:5:4)


**וְדָאַג.** איטוביטאר״ה בלע״ז:


**And worry.** *E dubitera* in O.F.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:6:1)


**אֶת דַּרְכֵּנוּ.** מַעֲשֵׂה הָאֲתוֹנוֹת מַה נִּהְיְתָה בָּם, שֶׁבִּשְׁבִילָם הָלַכְנוּ אֶת כָּל הַדֶּרֶךְ הַזֶּה:


**Our road.** The story about the donkeys, i.e., what has become of them, for whom we have gone all this way.²

footnotes:
² They were not only seeking advice where to look for the donkeys, but were also looking for an explanation for their long journey. They were puzzled over why they had searched and not found them yet, being that donkeys usually do not stray far from their home.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:7:1)


**וּמַה נָּבִיא לָאִישׁ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״אִם מְקַבֵּל מָמוֹן, מָה נְעוּל לְגַבְרָא״. לֹא הָיָה שָׁאוּל מַכִּיר בְּמַעֲשָׂיו שֶׁל שְׁמוּאֵל, וְסָבַר שֶׁיִּשְׁאַל שָׂכָר:


**What shall we bring to the man.** Targum Yonoson renders, 'If he accepts money, what shall we bring to the man?' Shaul was not familiar with Shmuel's habits and thought that he would ask for remuneration.³

footnotes:
³ It seems that in those days it was customary to bring gifts in this type of situation; as in II Melachim 4:42. They thought that perhaps Shmuel might demand some form of remuneration for his prophecies just as diviners seek gifts from those for whom they predict the future.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:7:2)


**וּתְשׁוּרָה.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וּמִדְעַם דְּכָשַׁר״, לְשׁוֹן יוֹשֶׁר. וּמְנַחֵם פֵּירַשׁ: ׳תְּשׁוּרָה׳, לְשׁוֹן תִּקְרֹבֶת, רְאוֹת פְּנֵי מֶלֶךְ וְאָדָם חָשׁוּב, לְשׁוֹן ״אֲשׁוּרֶנּוּ״ (במדבר כד:יז), וְנִרְאִין דְּבָרָיו:


**And [we have no] gift.** Targum Yonoson renders, 'and there is nothing proper.' This is an expression of יוֹשֶׁר [=propriety]. Menachem, however, explains תְּשׁוּרָה as an expression of a tribute for the privilege of seeing a king or an important person; [תְּשׁוּרָה being] an expression related to אֲשׁוּרֶנּוּ [meaning] 'I behold them,'⁴ and I agree with his explanation.⁵

footnotes:
⁴ Bamidbar 24:17.
⁵ That וּתְשׁוּרָה is from the root שוּר [=to see].



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:8:1)


**רֶבַע שֶׁקֶל.** ״זוּזָא חֲדָא דְּכַסְפָּא״:


**A quarter shekel.** One *zuz* of silver.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:9:1)


**לְפָנִים בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל.** הַסּוֹפֵר כָּתַב זֹאת, וְאֵין זֶה מִדִּבְרֵי נַעַר שָׁאוּל:


**In former times in Yisroel.** The author wrote this verse,⁶ i.e., this is not part of the youth of Shaul's statement.

footnotes:
⁶ I.e., Shmuel. See Maseches Bava Basra 14b which states that Shmuel authored the Book of Shmuel. However, being that Shmuel died before Shaul who only reigned for two years, what is the meaning of “formerly” and “of today” as stated in the verse? Radak therefore suggests that “formerly” refers to an earlier period when prophets were known for their ability to predict the future and to display a knowledge of hidden things, therefore the term רוֹאֶה. However, in Shmuel’s time prophecy became widespread, but the type of prophecy was a Divine inspiration to bring to the people the word of ‘ה, therefore the term נָבִיא [from the root בוֹא]. In Shaul’s case he was looking for a רוֹאֶה to tell him where his donkeys were. Others however, maintain that the author of this verse may have been Ezra or Yirmiyahu.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:9:2)


**כִּי לַנָּבִיא הַיּוֹם.** לְמִי שֶׁקּוֹרִים אוֹתוֹ נָבִיא הַיּוֹם, הָיוּ קוֹרִים לְפָנִים ׳רוֹאֶה׳:


**For the "prophet" of today.** The one who is called 'prophet' today was formerly called 'seer.'



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:1)


**כְּבֹאֲכֶם הָעִיר כֵּן וְגוֹ׳.** מַאֲרִיכוֹת הָיוּ בַּדְּבָרִים, כְּדֵי לְהִסְתַּכֵּל בְּיָפְיוֹ שֶׁל שָׁאוּל:


**As you enter the city you…** They prolonged their conversation in order to gaze upon Shaul's handsomeness.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ Maseches Berachos 48b. The girls gave an extraordinarily long answer to a relatively simple question. The fact that the entire conversation was recorded in Scripture makes it likely that they were struck by Shaul’s handsome appearance and wanted to gaze at him a few moments longer.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:2)


**הַבָּמָתָה.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״לְבֵית אִסְחָרוּתָא״. מִשֶׁחָרְבָה שִׁילֹה הֻתְּרוּ הַבָּמוֹת, וְהָיוּ מַקְרִיבִין שְׁלָמִים בַּבָּמָה, וְאוֹכְלִים יַחַד:


**The high place.** Targum Yonoson renders, 'to the banquet hall.' When Shiloh was destroyed, it became permissible [to offer sacrifices on] the high places, and they would sacrifice peace-offerings on the high place and eat together.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:3)


**כִּי הוּא יְבָרֵךְ הַזֶּבַח.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״אֲרֵי הוּא פָּרִיס מְזוֹנָא״, בּוֹצֵעַ עַל הַמָּזוֹן, עַל הַשְּׁלָמִים מְבָרְכִין בַּאֲכִילָתָם: בָּרוּךְ אַתָּה ה׳ אֱלֹהֵינוּ מֶלֶךְ הָעוֹלָם אֲשֶׁר קִדְּשָׁנוּ בְּמִצְוֹתָיו וְצִוָּנוּ לֶאֱכוֹל אֶת הַזֶּבַח:


**Because he blesses the offering.** Targum Yonoson renders, אֲרֵי הוּא פָּרִיס עַל מְזוֹנָא meaning, 'for he will break bread.' [Before] partaking of the peace-offering they recited: 'Blessed are You Adonoy, our God, King of the universe, Who has sanctified us with His commandments, and commanded us to eat the sacrifice."


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:13:4)


**כְּהַיּוֹם.** כַּאֲשֶׁר הַיּוֹם קַיָּם:


**As [surely as it is] day.** Just as the day exists.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ He said this to emphasize the truthfulness of his statement.—Radak



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:17:1)


**יַעְצֹר בְּעַמִּי.** יִמְשׁוֹל, כְּמוֹ ״עָצוּר וְעָזוּב״ (דברים לב:לו). כָּךְ דֶּרֶךְ הַמּוֹשֵׁל, לַעֲצֹר הָעָם בַּמִּלְחָמָה, שֶׁלֹּא יִתְפַּזְּרוּ וְלֹא יִתְפַּשְּׁטוּ זֶה מִזֶּה, וְלַעֲצֹר בְּכָל אֶחָד מֵעֲשׂוֹת רָעָה. בִּלְשׁוֹנֵנוּ קוֹרִין ׳מַעֲצוֹר׳, קירשטני״ר בלע״ז:


**Will rule over My people.** Will restrain [=will rule], similar to 'restrained [עָצוּר] and let free.'⁹ This is the function of the ruler, to restrain the people during war, that they not scatter or spread out one from another, and¹⁰ to restrain each one from doing evil.¹¹ In our language, we call מַעֲצוֹר*destenir,* in O.F.

footnotes:
⁹ Devarim 32:36.
¹⁰ In the time of peace…
¹¹ The phrase may be translated as “he will restrain them [the Philistines] from [attacing] My people”.—Radak



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:18:1)


**וַיִּגַּשׁ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וַעֲרַע״:


**Approached.** Targum Yonoson renders, 'met.'



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:20:1)


**וּלְמִי כָּל חֶמְדַּת יִשְׂרָאֵל.** כָּאן בָּא לִרְמוֹז לוֹ דְּבַר הַמְּלוּכָה:


**But for whom does all of Yisroel desire?** Here he [Shmuel] hints to him the matter of the kingship.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:22:1)


**בְּרֹאשׁ הַקְּרוּאִים.** בִּמְקוֹם מְסִבַּת הַגְּדוֹלִים, כִּי בְּדֶרֶךְ מְסִבָּתָן הָיָה נִכָּר אֵיזֶה מָקוֹם מֵסֵב הַגָּדוֹל:


**At the head of the invited guests.** In the place where the important people are seated; by the manner of the seating, it was obvious in which place the most important person was seated.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:23:1)


**אֲשֶׁר אָמַרְתִּי אֵלֶיךָ.** לְפִי שֶׁיָּדַע שְׁמוּאֵל שֶׁיָּבֹא שָׁאוּל, צִוָּה לָתֵת לוֹ מָנָה יָפָה לְצָרְכּוֹ:


**About which I told you.** Because Shmuel knew that Shaul would come, he ordered to give him a fine portion for his benefit.¹²

footnotes:
¹² When the special portion that Shmuel had set aside was placed before Shaul, it publicized the honor that Shmuel as giving him.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:1)


**אֶת הַשּׁוֹק וְהֶעָלֶיהָ.** אֶת הַשּׁוֹק וְהַיָּרֵךְ שֶׁעָלֶיהָ:


**The thigh with what was attached to it.** The thigh and the hip above it.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ See Maseches Avodah Zara 25a for differing views of “what was attached to it.”


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:2)


**הִנֵּה הַנִּשְׁאָר.** מִן הַמָּנוֹת שֶׁל הַקְּרוּאִים, וְהוּצַּב לְבַד לְצָרְכְּךָ:


**Here is what remains.** From the portions of the invited guests, and has been set aside for you.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:3)


**שִׂים לְפָנֶיךָ אֱכֹל.** בְּפֶרֶק אַחֲרוֹן דִּזְבָחִים (קכ א): קַיְּמָא לָן חָזֶה וָשׁוֹק בְּבָמָה גְּדוֹלָה, וְאֵין חָזֶה וָשׁוֹק בְּבָמָה קְטַנָּה, לְפִיכָךְ הֻתַּר לֶאֱכוֹל לְשָׁאוּל:


**Put before you and eat.** In the final chapter of [Maseches] Zevochim,¹⁴ it is established that [the waving of] the breast and thigh is [performed] in a large [i.e., public] high place, [but the waving of] the breast and thigh is not [performed] in a small [i.e., private] high place. It was therefore permissible for Shaul to eat¹⁵ [the thigh].

footnotes:
¹⁵ The phrase “put before you and eat” indicates that at first Shaul hesitated to eat because it was his opinion that his portion was the kohein’s due. Shmuel therefore told him that in a private high place, the breast and thigh were neither waved nor given to the kohein.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:24:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:4)


**כִּי לַמּוֹעֵד שָׁמוּר.** כִּי לְמוֹעֵד הָאֲכִילָה הָיָה הַשּׁוֹק הַזֶּה שָׁמוּר לְצָרְכְּךָ:


**For it was kept… until the appointed time.** Because for the appointed time of the banquet, this thigh had been reserved for you.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:24:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:24:5)


**לֵאמֹר הָעָם קָרָאתִי.** אֲמִירָתִי הָיְתָה לַטַּבָּח: הָעָם קָרָאתִי לִסְעוּדָה, וְיוֹדֵעַ אֲנִי מִסְפָּרָם, וְהִזָּהֵר בְּמָנוֹתָם, וְנָתַתִּי לוֹ הַמָּנוֹת לְמִסְפָּרָם, וְזוֹ שְׁמוּרָה לְצָרְכְּךָ:


**I told the [cook], I have invited the people.** My statement to the cook was, 'I invited the people to the feast, and I know their number. Be careful with their portions.' And I ordered from him the number of portions accordingly, and this one was reserved for you.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:25:1)


**וַיְדַבֵּר עִם שָׁאוּל עַל הַגָּג.** מוֹכִיחוֹ וּמְלַמְּדוֹ לְיִרְאָה אֶת הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא:


**And he spoke with Shaul on the roof.** Reproving him¹⁶ and teaching him to fear the Holy One, Blessed is He.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Based on Maseches Makos 11a that states that using the form of וַיְדַבֵּר for describing speech indicates harshness.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:27:1)


**וְיַעֲבֹר לְפָנֵינוּ.** וְיֵלֵךְ בְּרָחוֹק מִמֶּנּוּ, וְלֹא יִשְׁמַע אֶת דְּבָרֵינוּ:


**To pass ahead of us.** To go at a distance from us so that he will not hear our conversation.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 9:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/9:27:2)


**וַיַּעֲבֹר.** הַנַּעַר לִפְנֵיהֶם, וּלְשָׁאוּל אָמַר: וְאַתָּה עֲמֹד עִמָּדִי:


**And he went ahead.** The youth went ahead of them, but to Shaul he said, 'And you stand with me.'



## Chapter 10


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:2:1)


**עִם קְבֻרַת רָחֵל בִּגְבוּל בִּנְיָמִן.** וַהֲלֹא קְבוּרַת רָחֵל בִּגְבוּל יְהוּדָה בְּבֵית לֶחֶם, אֶלָּא עַכְשָׁיו הֵם בִּקְבוּרַת רָחֵל, וּכְשֶׁתִּפְגַּע בָּהֶם תִּמְצָאֵם בִּגְבוּל בִּנְיָמִן בְּצֶלְצַח, כָּךְ שְׁנוּיָה בְּתוֹסֶפְתָּא דְּסוֹטָה (פרק יא):


**By Rochel's Tomb, at the border of Binyomin.** Isn't Rochel's Tomb within Yehudah's border, in Beis Lechem? [The answer is that] they are now by Rochel's Tomb, but, 'when you will meet them, you will find them within Binyomin's border, in Tzeltzoch.' Thus was taught by the Tosefta of [Maseches] Sotah.¹

footnotes:
¹ 11:7.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:2:2)


**צֶלְצַח.** צֵל לַצַּח שֶׁל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, שֶׁהוּא צַח וְאָדוֹם, וְהִיא יְרוּשָׁלַיִם:


Tzeltzoch is a combination of the words צֵל לַצַּח of the Holy One, Blessed is He, Who is pure and ruddy;² and this is Yerusholayim.

footnotes:
² Shir Hashirim 5:10.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:2:3)


**וְדָאַג לָכֶם.** יִירָא עֲלֵיכֶם, כָּל לְשׁוֹן ׳דְּאָגָה׳, לְשׁוֹן ׳יִרְאָה׳, דושטי״ר בלע״ז:


**And is worrying about you.** Has fears concerning you; every expression of דְּאָגָה [worry] is an expression of fear; *dementer* in O.F.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:3:1)


**אֵלוֹן תָּבוֹר.** ״מֵישַׁר תָּבוֹר״:


**Eilon Tavor.** The plain of Tavor.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:1)


**גִּבְעַת הָאֱלֹהִים.** גִּבְעַת קִרְיַת יְעָרִים, אֲשֶׁר עַכְשָׁיו אֲרוֹן הָאֱלֹהִים שָׁם:


**The hill of God.** The hill of Kiryas Ye'arim, where the Ark of God was now resting.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:2)


**נְצִיבֵי פְלִשְׁתִּים.** סַרְדִּיוֹטוֹת שֶׁמִּנּוּ עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁהָיוּ רוֹדִין בָּהּ, וּמוֹשִׁיבִים נְצִיבִים בְּעָרֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**Philistine officers.** Officers whom they had appointed over Yisroel, for they [the Philistines] were ruling over them and stationing officers in the cities of Yisroel.³

footnotes:
³ The Philistines had been conquering parts of Eretz Yisroel and had appointed officers to govern the areas that they dominated.—Mahri Kra


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:3)


**חֶבֶל נְבִיאִים.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״סִיעַת סָפְרַיָּא״. **חֶבֶל:** סִיעָה, וְכֵן ״חֶבְלֵי שְׁאוֹל סְבָבוּנִי״ (שמואל ב כב:ו), ״מַשְׁרְיַת רְשִׁיעַיָּא אַקְפוּנִי״:


**A band of prophets.** Targum Yonoson renders, 'a band of scribes.' 'חֶבֶל' is a band, similar to, 'the חֶבְלֵי of the wicked surrounded me'⁴ [rendered by Targum as], 'the bands of the wicked surrounded me.'

footnotes:
⁴ II Shmuel 22:6.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:5:4)


**מֵהַבָּמָה.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״מִבֵּית אִסְחָרוּתָא״:


**From the high place.** Targum Yonoson renders, 'from the banquet hall.'



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:7:1)


**כִּי תָבֹאנָה הָאֹתוֹת הָאֵלֶּה לָךְ.** וְתֵדַע כִּי דְּבָרַי אֱמֶת, וּכְשֵׁם שֶׁבָּאוּ לְךָ אוֹתוֹת הַלָּלוּ, כָּךְ יִתְקַיֵּם דְּבַר הַמְּלוּכָה:


**And when these signs come upon you.** And you will realize that my words are true; for just as these signs will come to you, so will the matter of the kingship be fulfilled.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ And therefore “prepare yourself…”


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:7:2)


**עֲשֵׂה לְךָ אֲשֶׁר תִּמְצָא יָדֶךָ.** ״אַתְקִין לָךְ מָאנֵי מַלְכוּתָא״, וְהָכֵן לְךָ טַכְסִיסֵי הַמְּלוּכָה כְּמוֹ שֶׁתּוּכַל:


**Prepare yourself as well as you can.** [Targum renders,] 'prepare for yourself regal garments, and make ready for yourself the court ritual,' as well as you can.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:8:1)


**וְיָרַדְתָּ לְפָנַי הַגִּלְגָּל.** וּלְאַחַר שֶׁתִּמְלוֹךְ, תֵּרֵד הַגִּלְגָּל לְפָנַי, קֹדֶם שֶׁאֵרֵד אֲנִי:


**Then you shall go down to Gilgol ahead of me.** After you reign, you shall come down to Gilgol before me, i.e., before I go down.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:8:2)


**וְהִנֵּה אָנֹכִי יֹרֵד אֵלֶיךָ.** לְסוֹף שִׁבְעַת יָמִים שֶׁתֵּרֵד אַתָּה:


**And I will go down to you.** At the end of seven days after you will have gone down.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:8:3)


**תּוֹחֵל.** תַּמְתִּין:


**Wait.** You shall wait.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:9:1)


**לֵב אַחֵר.** רוּחַ גְּבוּרַת מַלְכוּת:


**Transformed his heart.** [To] a spirit of royal power.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:12:1)


**וּמִי אֲבִיהֶם.** מָה תֵּימַהּ לְךָ בַּדָּבָר מִי אֲבִיהֶם שֶׁל נְבִיאִים, וְכִי נְבוּאָה יְרוּשָׁה הִיא:


**And who is their father?** Why do you wonder about the matter as to who is the father of the prophets? Is prophecy hereditary?⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Or וּמִי אֲבִיהֶם can mean “Who is their teacher?” for one’s teacher is called his father as in II Melachim 2:12. He implied to the people that ‘ה is their teacher and just as He teaches the other prophets, He can teach Shaul too.—Radak



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:16:1)


**וְאֶת דְּבַר הַמְּלוּכָה לֹא הִגִּיד.** דֶּרֶךְ צְנִיעוּת הָיְתָה בּוֹ:


**But about the matter of the kingship he did not tell him.** He possessed a discretionary nature.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ See Maseches Megillah 13b.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:21:1)


**וַיִּלָּכֵד שָׁאוּל.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה שָׁם, נָפַל הַגּוֹרָל עָלָיו, שֶׁהָיוּ כְּתוּבִין שְׁמָם בְּאִגְּרוֹת וּנְתוּנִין בְּקֻפְסָא, וְהַנָּבִיא מַכְנִיס יָדוֹ וְנוֹטֵל אִגֶּרֶת אַחַת:


**And Shaul… was singled out.** Even though he was not there, the lot fell upon him, for their names were written on pieces of parchment and placed in a box. The prophet would insert his hand and take one piece of parchment.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:22:1)


**נֶחְבָּא אֶל הַכֵּלִים.** בַּבַּיִת שֶׁנָּתְנוּ שָׁם הַבָּאִים כְּלֵיהֶן, שֶׁהָיָה בּוֹרֵחַ מִן הַגְּדוּלָּה. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא: וְאֶל הַכֵּלִים, בָּאוּרִים וְתוּמִּים, אָמַר: שֶׁמָּא אֵינִי רָאוּי לִגְדֻלָּה עַד שֶׁיִּשְׁאֲלוּ בָּאוּרִים וְתֻמִּים, וְשָׁאֲלוּ וְעָלָה בְּיָדוֹ:


**Is hidden by the baggage.** In the room where newcomers had placed their baggage; [he hid] because he was running away from the high position. But the Midrash Aggadah of R. Tanchuma⁸ explains that 'אֶל הַכֵּלִים' refers to the Urim and Tumim. He said [to himself], 'perhaps I am not worthy of this high position. [I will not assume it]. until they inquire of the Urim and Tumim.⁹ Thereupon they asked, and he accepted it.

footnotes:
⁸ Vayikra 3.
⁹ Shaul was convinced that the Urim and Tumim would confirm that he was not worthy of the kingship. But, on the contrary, it confirmed that nobody was equal to him. In Maseches Eiruvin 13b it states that he who runs away from honor is rightfully pursued by it.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Samuel 10:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/10:26:1)


**אֲשֶׁר נָגַע אֱלֹהִים.** ״גֻּבְרִין דַּחֲלֵי חֲטָאָה דְּאִתְיְהֵב דַּחֲלָא מִן קֳדָם ה׳ בְּלִבְּהוֹן״:


**God had touched.** [Targum renders], 'men who fear sin, in whose heart 'ה has instilled fear.'



## Chapter 11


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:2:1)


**כָּל עַיִן יָמִין.** כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ, וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה (ילקוט שמעוני רמז קיא): הָבִיאוּ לִי סֵפֶר תּוֹרַתְכֶם שֶׁנִּתְּנָה מִיָּמִין, וְאֶשְׂרְפָהּ, עַל שֶׁכָּתוּב בּוֹ (דברים כג:ד): ״לֹא יָבֹא עַמּוֹנִי וּמוֹאָבִי וְגוֹ׳״:


**Each right eye.** Explain it literally.¹ But the Midrash Aggadah [explains it figuratively, that he said,] "Bring me your Torah scroll which was given by the right hand [of God],² and I will burn it, because He wrote in it, "Neither an Ammonite nor a Moavite may enter [into Adonoy's community]."³

footnotes:
¹ As a symbol of everlasting defeat.
² See Devarim 33:2.
³ Devarim 23:4. Therefore, Nochosh said that he cannot accept any form of peace treaty with the Bnei Yisroel when the Torah specifically forbids to seek peace with Ammon. In Devarim 23:7 the Torah stated with regard to Ammon and Moav, “You shall not seek their peace or welfare, all your days, forever.” Nochosh therefore wanted to burn the Torah.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:2:2)


**חֶרְפָּה.** גִּדּוּף:


**A sign of shame.** An insult.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:5:1)


**אַחֲרֵי הַבָּקָר.** אַחַר זְמַן מוֹעֵד בִּיאַת הַבָּקָר מִן הַשָּׂדֶה:


**Behind the cattle.** After the appointed time that the cattle come in from the field.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:7:1)


**לִבְקָרוֹ.** לִבְהֵמוֹתָיו:


**To his cattle.** To his animals.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:8:1)


**וַיִּפְקְדֵם בְּבָזֶק.** רַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (יומא כב:): בְּשִׁבְרֵי חֲרָסִים (ס״א, אינו). לָשׁוֹן אַחֵר: בְּבָזֶק, בַּאֲבָנִים, שֶׁנָּטַל מִכָּל אֶחָד אֶבֶן וּמְנָאָם, כְּמוֹ (שמואל א טו:ד): ״וַיִּפְקְדֵם בַּטְּלָאִים״, שֶׁנָּטַל מִכָּל אֶחָד טָלֶה וּמְנָאָם בָּהֶם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהָיוּ מוֹנִים אוֹתָם בַּחֲצָאֵי שְׁקָלִים, וְתִרְגֵּם (משלי כו:ח): ״כִּצְרוֹר אֶבֶן בְּמַרְגֵּמָה״, ״הֵיךְ בִּזְקָא דְכֵיפָא בְּקַלְעָא״. וּבִילַמְּדֵנוּ: וַיִּפְקְדֵם בַּטְּלָאִים, כַּד אִינוּן מִסְכְּנִין, בְּאִלֵּין בִּזְקַיָּא, כַּד אִינוּן עֲתִירִים, בְּאִלֵּין אִימְרַיָּא. לָשׁוֹן אַחֵר: ׳בְּבָזֶק׳, שֵׁם מָקוֹם הַנִּזְכָּר בְּסֵפֶר שׁוֹפְטִים (א:ה) ״וַיִּמְצְאוּ אֶת אֲדֹנִי בֶזֶק בְּבָזֶק״:


**He counted them in Bezek.** Our Rabbis said that he counted them using earthenware shards.⁴ Another explanation is that בְּבָזֶק means 'with pebbles,' i.e., he took a pebble from each one, and counted them, similar to, "And he counted them with lambs,"⁵ i.e., he took a lamb from each one, and counted them with the lambs, just as they would count them with half shekels.⁶ Targum reders, in the verse, "as one who loads a pebble [אֶבֶן] for throwing in a slingshot,"⁷ as one who loads a בִּזְקָא into a slingshot. And in [Midrash] Yelam'denu, "And he counted them with lambs," [is explained as,] "when they were poor, [they were counted] with pebbles and when they became rich, [they were counted] with lambs. Another explanation is that בָזֶק is the name of a place, mentioned in Shoftim, "they found Adoni Bezek in Bezek."⁸

footnotes:
⁴ See Maseches Yoma 22b. According to this rendering, Bezek is not a name of a place, but rather a description of the means used by which to count the people.
⁵ Below 15:4.
⁶ See Rashi Ibid.
⁷ Mishlei 26:8.
⁸ Shoftim 1:5.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:12:1)


**מִי הָאֹמֵר.** בִּלְשׁוֹן בִּזָּיוֹן, שָׁאוּל יִמְלֹךְ עָלֵינוּ, תְּנוּ אוֹתָם וּנְמִיתֵם:


**Who is it that says.** Derisively, "Shaul will reign over us?" Give them over and we will execute them.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:12:2)


**יִמְלֹךְ עָלֵינוּ.** בִּתְמִיהָ:


**Will reign over us.** As a question.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 11:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/11:14:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר שְׁמוּאֵל וְגוֹ׳ וּנְחַדֵּשׁ שָׁם הַמְּלוּכָה.** לְפִי שֶׁבָּרִאשׁוֹנָה הָיוּ עוֹרְרִים עַל הַדָּבָר, וְעַתָּה נִתְרַצּוּ כֻּלָּם:


**And Shmuel said, "…and renew the kingdom there."** Because at first there was opposition in the matter, but now they unanimously agreed.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Shmuel merely invited them to Gilgol; it was not a command. See above 9:9 for a similar expression וְנֵלְכָה. However, now that Shaul saved the people of Yoveish Gilad from the Ammonites; all the people rallied behind him and accepted him as their king.



## Chapter 12


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:2:1)


**וַאֲנִי זָקַנְתִּי וָשַׂבְתִּי.** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ: זִקְנָה קָפְצָה עָלָיו, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּמַסֶּכֶת תַּעֲנִית (ה:ב):


**I have aged and become gray.** Our Rabbis said that he aged prematurely, as stated in Maseches Ta'anis.¹

footnotes:
¹ 5b.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:1)


**אֶת שׁוֹר מִי לָקַחְתִּי.** לַעֲבוֹדָתִי:


**Whose ox have I taken?** For my work.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:2)


**וַחֲמוֹר מִי לָקַחְתִּי.** כְּשֶׁהָיִיתִי הוֹלֵךְ מֵעִיר לְעִיר לִשְׁפּוֹט אוֹתָם עַל עִסְקֵי צָרְכֵיהֶם, הָיִיתִי הוֹלֵךְ עַל הַחֲמוֹר שֶׁלִּי, וְהָיָה לִי לִטּוֹל מִשֶּׁלָּהֶם:


**Whose donkey have I taken?** When I used to travel from city to city to judge them concerning their matters, I used to travel on my donkey, although I should have taken theirs.²

footnotes:
² See Bamidbar Rabbah 18:10. And when I brought a sacrifice for their sake, I should have taken their ox. Shmuel took this extra precaution because he witnessed how severely Eili’s sons were punished for having abused the privileges of their office. He therefore avoided any action that could be misconstrued as resembling their behavior.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:3)


**אֶת מִי רַצּוֹתִי.** כָּל לְשׁוֹן מְרַצֶּה הַסָּמוּךְ לְעֹשֶׁק, לְשׁוֹן עָשׁוּק וְרָצוּץ הוּא, חוֹמֵס דַּלִּים וְרוֹצֵץ:


**Whom have I oppressed?** Every expression of מְרַצֶּה which follows עשֶׁק [robbery], is an expression of "robbed and oppressed,³ one who robs the poor and oppresses them.

footnotes:
³ Devarim 28:33.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:4)


**וְאַעְלִים עֵינַי בּוֹ.** כְּדֵי לְהַעֲלִים עֵינַי מִן הַמִּשְׁפָּט בִּשְׁבִיל הַמָּמוֹן:


**To look away from him.** In order to hide my eyes from the judgment because of the money [bribe].


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:3:5)


**וְאָשִׁיב לָכֶם.** כָּל מַה שֶּׁתֹּאמְרוּ:


**And I will give it back to you.** Whatever you will say.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:5:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר עֵד.** בַּת קוֹל יוֹצֵאת וְאוֹמֶרֶת: ׳עֵד׳, וְזֶה אֶחָד מִשְּׁלֹשָׁה מְקוֹמוֹת שֶׁהוֹפִיעָה רוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ בְּבֵית דִּין שֶׁל מַטָּה, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּמַסֶּכֶת מַכּוֹת (כג:ב):


**And they said [as one], "Witnessed."** A heavenly voice came out and said, '[I am] a witness.' This is one of three places in which the holy spirit entered the earthly court, as it is stated in Maseches Makos.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ 23b. The other two places in which the holy spirit entered the earthly court are in the court of Sheim and in the court of Shlomo.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:6:1)


**אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה אֶת מֹשֶׁה וְאֶת אַהֲרֹן.** לִהְיוֹת נְכוֹנִים לִשְׁלִיחוּתוֹ, לְהוֹצִיא אֲבוֹתֵיכֶם מִמִּצְרָיִם:


**Who produced Moshe and Aharon.** To be prepared for His mission to bring your forefathers out of Egypt.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:7:1)


**וְאִשָּׁפְטָה אִתְּכֶם.** אֶתְוַכַּח עִמָּכֶם:


**And I shall enter into judgment.** I.e., I shall debate with you.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:11:1)


**יְרֻבַּעַל.** זֶה גִּדְעוֹן:


****יְרֻבַּעַל. This is Gideon.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Shoftim 6:32. See Maseches Rosh Hashanah 25a.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:11:2)


**בְּדָן.** זֶה שִׁמְשׁוֹן, שֶׁמִּשֵּׁבֶט דָּן בָּא:


**Bedan.** This is Shimshon who came from the tribe of Don.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ בְּדָן=“in Don” or בֶּן דָן=בְּדָן meaning “a member of Don’s tribe.”


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:11:3)


**וְאֶת יִפְתָּח.** הֲרֵי שְׁלֹשָׁה קַלֵּי עוֹלָם, עִם שְׁלֹשָׁה חֲמוּרֵי עוֹלָם, מֹשֶׁה וְאַהֲרֹן וּשְׁמוּאֵל, לוֹמַר לְךָ, הַקַּל בְּדוֹרוֹ, כֶּחָמוּר בְּדוֹרוֹ, כָּל בֵּית דִּין הַמִּתְמַנֶּה עַל הַדּוֹר, צָרִיךְ לֵילֵךְ אַחֲרָיו כְּאִלּוּ הוּא אַבִּיר שֶׁבָּאַבִּירִים:


**And Yiphtoch.** Here are three insignificant leaders with three world-important leaders: Moshe, Aharon and Shmuel, to teach you that the insignificant leader in his generation is equal to the important leader in his generation. Every court which is appointed over the generation, must be followed as though its [members] were the mightiest of the nobility.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Ibid., 25b. Shmuel indicated to the people that every generation must regard its sages and leaders with the same respect they would give to Moshe, Aharon or Shmuel. Therefore, Yiphtoch in his generation was equal to Moshe in his generation. See Rashi in Devarim 19:17.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:14:1)


**וִהְיִתֶם גַּם אַתֶּם.** וְתִתְקַיְּמוּ לְאֹרֶךְ יָמִים גַּם אַתֶּם גַּם הַמֶּלֶךְ:


**Then both you.** You will live to a ripe old age, both you and the king.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:15:1)


**וְהָיְתָה יַד ה׳ בָּכֶם וּבַאֲבֹתֵיכֶם.** כְּלוֹמַר וְהָיְתָה בָּכֶם אַחַר שֶׁהָיְתָה בַּאֲבוֹתֵיכֶם, וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (יבמות סג ב): בָּכֶם וּבַאֲבוֹתֵיכֶם, חִטּוּטֵי שְׁכָבֵי, וְהִיא מַכַּת בִּזָּיוֹן עַל הַמֵּתִים:


**Then Adonoy's hand will be against you and against your forefathers.** Meaning it will be against you after it was against your fathers. Our Rabbis however said, "against you and against your fathers" refers to the digging up of the dead, which is a disgrace upon the dead.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Maseches Yevamos 63b. When people sin, the past generations are also punished by having their grave sites disturbed.—Radak



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:16:1)


**הִתְיַצְּבוּ וּרְאוּ וְגוֹ׳.** וּכְשֵׁם שֶׁעַל יְדֵי תְּפִלָּתִי אֲנִי יָכוֹל לְשַׁנּוֹת אֶת הָעִתִּים, כָּךְ אִם פָּגְעָה בָּכֶם מִלְחָמָה, הָיָה כֹּחַ בִּתְפִלָּתִי לַעֲמֹד עַל הָאוֹיֵב, וְלֹא הֱיִיתֶם צְרִיכִים לִשְׁאֹל מֶלֶךְ בְּחַיַּי, וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֲנִי זָקֵן:


**Stand up and see…** And just as through my prayer, I am able to change the seasons, similarly, if a war befell you, my prayer would have the power to protect you against the enemy, and you had no need to ask for a king during my lifetime,¹⁰ even though I am old.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Shmuel demonstrated to the nation how unnecessary their request for a king was. Just as ‘ה responded immediately to Shmuel’s prayers for rain, so too would ‘ה answer his prayers to save the Bnei Yisroel from their enemies.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:17:1)


**הֲלֹא קְצִיר חִטִּים הַיּוֹם.** וְהַגְּשָׁמִים סִימַן קְלָלָה, וְקָשֶׁה בְּעֵינֵי הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא לְהָבִיא פֻּרְעָנוּת חִנָּם, אַף עַל פִּי כֵן יֵשׁ בִּי כֹּחַ, וְאֶקְרָא אֶל ה׳ וְיִתֵּן קוֹלוֹת וּמָטָר:


**Is today not the [season of the] wheat harvest?** And rain is a sign of a curse,¹¹ and the Holy One, Blessed is He, is reluctant to visit punishment unnecessarily. Nonetheless, I have power, and I shall call to 'ה, and He will make it thunder and rain.

footnotes:
¹¹ Maseches Ta’anis 12b.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:17:2)


**וּדְעוּ וּרְאוּ.** כִּי לֹא הֱיִיתֶם צְרִיכִים לִשְׁאֹל מֶלֶךְ לְזַלְזֵל בִּי:


**Then you will know and see.** That it was unnecessary for you to ask for a king and to show disrespect towards me.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:22:1)


**בַּעֲבוּר שְׁמוֹ הַגָּדוֹל.** אֲשֶׁר יָצָא שְׁמוֹ עֲלֵיכֶם שֶׁהוּא מוֹשִׁיעֲכֶם, וּלְמַעַן לֹא יִתְמַעֵט שֵׁם גָּדְלוֹ:


**For the sake of His great Name.** For His fame has spread because of you, that He is your savior, and lest the fame of His greatness be lessened.¹²

footnotes:
¹² This verse indicates that ‘ה will not abandon the Bnei Yisroel because of the lack of their own merits, in order to uphold His own Name.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:22:2)


**הוֹאִיל ה׳.** נִשְׁבַּע, כְּמוֹ (שמואל א יד:כד): ״וַיֹּאֶל שָׁאוּל אֶת הָעָם״:


**Adonoy has sworn.**הוֹאִיל meaning he swore, as in, "And Shaul made the people swear ]וַיֹאֶל[."¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Below 14:24.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Samuel 12:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/12:23:1)


**גַּם אָנֹכִי חָלִילָה לִי.** מֵאַחַר שֶׁתַּעֲשׂוּ אַתֶּם אֶת שֶׁלָּכֶם לָשׁוּב אֶל ה׳, גַּם אָנֹכִי חָלִילָה לִּי מִחֲדֹל מֵהִתְפַּלֵּל בַּעַדְכֶם:


**And as for me, far be it from me.** Since you will do your part by returning to 'ה, I also, far be it from me to stop praying on your behalf.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ From this verse, the Gemara in Maseches Berachos 12b, derives, that if a person knows that his prayers will be answered if he prays for mercy for his fellow but refrains from doing so, he is considered to have sinned.



## Chapter 13


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:1:1)


**בֶּן שָׁנָה שָׁאוּל בְּמָלְכוֹ.** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ ז״ל (יומא כב ב): כְּבֶן שָׁנָה, שֶׁלֹּא טָעַם טַעַם חֵטְא. וְיֵשׁ לִפְתּוֹר: בֶּן שָׁנָה שָׁאוּל בְּמָלְכוֹ, בְּשָׁנָה רִאשׁוֹנָה שֶׁהוּמְלַךְ בָּהּ, וְהוּא מָלַךְ שְׁתֵּי שָׁנִים עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, וּבַשָּׁנָה רִאשׁוֹנָה, מִיָּד: ״וַיִּבְחַר לוֹ שָׁאוּל שְׁלֹשֶׁת אֲלָפִים״:


**One year was Shaul in his reign.** Our Rabbis, of sainted memory, said,¹ [Shaul was] like a one year old, who did not experience the taste of sin. It may also be interpreted, "Shaul was a year in his reign," i.e., in the first year in which he was made king, though he actually ruled over Yisroel two years, in the first year, immediately, "Shaul chose for himself three thousand [men]."

footnotes:
¹ Maseches Yoma 22b.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:3:1)


**אֶת נְצִיב פְּלִשְׁתִּים.** סַרְדְּיוֹט שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְהוֹשִׁיבוּהוּ בְּגִבְעַת בִּנְיָמִן:


**The Philistine governor.** They had a government official [who ruled] over Yisroel and they stationed him in Givas Binyomin.²

footnotes:
² Yonoson assassinated him as an open act of rebellion against the Philistines.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:3:2)


**יִשְׁמְעוּ הָעִבְרִים.** שֶׁמָּרַדְנוּ בַּפְּלִשְׁתִּים, וְיִשָּׁמְרוּ מֵהֶם:


**Let the Hebrews hear.** That we have rebelled against the Philistines and they should beware of them.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:4:1)


**וְגַם נִבְאַשׁ יִשְׂרָאֵל.** נִבְאַשׁ רֵיחָם בַּפְּלִשְׁתִּים, לְשׁוֹן ׳שִׂנְאָה׳:


**And that Yisroel has become hateful.** Literally, they have come in bad odor with the Philistines, an expression of hatred.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:4:2)


**אַחֲרֵי שָׁאוּל הַגִּלְגָּל.** הוּא שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ שְׁמוּאֵל (שמואל א י:ח) ״וְיָרַדְתָּ לְפָנַי הַגִּלְגָּל״:


**To follow Shaul to Gilgol.** This is what Shmuel had said to him, "you shall go down to Gilgol ahead of me."³

footnotes:
³ Above 10:8.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:5:1)


**קִדְמַת בֵּית אָוֶן.** בְּמִזְרַח בֵּית אָוֶן:


**To the east of Beis Oven.** [קִדְמַת] meaning the east of Beis Oven.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:6:1)


**וּבַחוֹחִים.** מְקוֹם קְבוּצַת חוֹחִים, שפנ״י בלע״ז:


**In thorn bushes.** A place where thorns are concentrated; *espineid* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:6:2)


**וּבַצְּרִחִים.** פליישי״ץ בלע״ז:


**In the towers.** *Pleisiz* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:6:3)


**וּבַבֹּרוֹת.** גֻּמּוֹת:


**And in pits.** Cisterns.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:7:1)


**עָבְרוּ אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן אֶרֶץ גָּד.** לִבְרֹחַ מִפְּנֵי פְּלִשְׁתִּים, שֶׁהַפְּלִשְׁתִּים הָיוּ בְּאֶרֶץ כְּנַעַן בְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן יָמָּה:


**Crossed the Yardein to the land of Gad.** To escape from the Philistines, because the Philistines were in the land of Canaan on the west side of the Yardein.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:7:2)


**חָרְדוּ אַחֲרָיו.** מִהֲרוּ לָלֶכֶת אַחֲרָיו:


**Hurried after him.** They hurried to go after him.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ I.e., to join forces with Shaul.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:8:1)


**וַיּוֹחֶל.** הִמְתִּין:


**He waited.** He waited.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ See Bereishis 8:10.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:8:2)


**לַמּוֹעֵד אֲשֶׁר שְׁמוּאֵל.** הֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא חָסֵר תֵּבָה אַחַת, ׳לַמּוֹעֵד אֲשֶׁר שָׂם שְׁמוּאֵל׳ אוֹ ׳לַמּוֹעֵד אֲשֶׁר לִשְׁמוּאֵל׳, וְדוּגְמָא לוֹ (שמואל ב ד:ב): ״וּשְׁנֵי אֲנָשִׁים שָׂרֵי גְדוּדִים הָיוּ בֶן שָׁאוּל״, הָיָה לוֹ לִכְתֹּב ׳לְבֶן שָׁאוּל׳, אַף כָּאן ׳אֲשֶׁר לִשְׁמוּאֵל׳, שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ (שמואל א י:ח) ״שִׁבְעַת יָמִים תּוֹחֵל עַד בּוֹאִי אֵלֶיךָ״:


**Till the time set by Shmuel.** This verse has a missing word. [The verse means to say], "till the appointed time set by Shmuel," or "to the appointed time of Shmuel." An example of this is, "And two men, officers of troops were the son of Shaul." Scripture should have written, "were to the son of Shaul."⁶ Here too, "which was set by Shmuel," who had said to him, "Wait seven days until I come to you."⁷

footnotes:
⁶ II Shmuel 4:2.
⁷ Above 10:8.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:9:1)


**הַגִּשׁוּ אֵלַי הָעֹלָה.** זָר מֻתָּר לְהַקְרִיב בַּבָּמָה:


**Bring me the burnt-offering.** A non-kohein is permitted to sacrifice on a high place.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:11:1)


**מֶה עָשִׂיתָ.** הֲלֹא אָמַרְתִּי לְךָ ״הִנְנִי יוֹרֵד אֵלֶיךָ לְהַעֲלוֹת הָעֹלָה״, אֲנִי אַעֲלֶה אוֹתָהּ, וְלֹא אַתָּה:


**What have you done?** Did I not say to you, "I will go down to you to offer the burnt-offering?" I will offer it up, and not you.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:11:2)


**לְמוֹעֵד הַיָּמִים.** בִּתְחִלַּת הַיּוֹם:


**At the appointed day.** At the beginning of the [appointed] day.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:12:1)


**וָאֶתְאַפַּק.** נִתְחַזַּקְתִּי עַל רְצוֹנִי, שֶׁהָיָה לִבִּי אוֹמֵר לְהַמְתִּין לְךָ, עַל כָּרְחִי הֶעֱמַדְתִּי אֶת לִבִּי וָאַעֲלֶה הָעֹלָה:


**So I forced myself.** I overcame my will, for my heart told me to wait for you, and against my will I suppressed my heart, and offered the burnt-offering.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Shaul realized that it was not proper for him to offer the sacrifice. But he saw that the people who had gathered became impatient and began to desert him for his not taking action. Therefore, in desperation he overcame his inclination to wait longer for Shmuel’s arrival and acted against his better judgement.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:13:1)


**כִּי עַתָּה.** לִפְנֵי עֲשׂוֹתְךָ זֹאת:


**For now.** Before you had done this,


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:13:2)


**הֵכִין ה׳ אֶת מַמְלַכְתְּךָ עַד עוֹלָם.** שֶׁכְּשֶׁפּוֹסְקִין לוֹ גְּדֻלָּה לְאָדָם, פּוֹסְקִין לוֹ וּלְדוֹרוֹתָיו (מגילה יג ב):


**Adonoy would have established your kingship … forever.** Because when a high position is assigned to a person, it is assigned to him and to his descendants.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ See Maseches Megillah 13b.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:14:1)


**וְעַתָּה מַמְלַכְתְּךָ לֹא תָקוּם.** שֶׁכֵּן אָמַר מֹשֶׁה בַּתּוֹרָה (דברים יז:כ): ״לְבִלְתִּי סוּר מִן הַמִּצְוָה יָמִין וּשְׂמֹאל לְמַעַן יַאֲרִיךְ יָמִים וְגוֹ׳״, הָא אִם סָר, לֹא יַאֲרִיךְ יָמִים, וְאִם תֹּאמַר לֹא עַל מִצְוַת נָבִיא נֶאֱמַר, אֶלָּא עַל מִצְוַת הַתּוֹרָה, הֲרֵי כְּבָר נֶאֱמַר (שם): ״לִשְׁמֹר אֶת כָּל דִּבְרֵי הַתּוֹרָה הַזֹּאת״, וְאַחַר כָּךְ: ״לְבִלְתִּי רוּם לְבָבוֹ וּלְבִלְתִּי סוּר מִן הַמִּצְוָה״, אֲפִלּוּ מִמִּצְוַת נָבִיא:


**But now, your kingship shall not endure.** Because Moshe said so in the Torah, "[and] he does not stray from the commandment right or left, so that days of his reign are lengthy…."¹⁰ Hence, if he does stray, the days [of his reign] will not be lengthy. And if you will argue that this was not said in reference to a Prophet's commandment, but only in reference to a commandment of the Torah, [I will answer that] it was already stated, "[and] to guard every word of this Torah" and afterwards, "that his pride not increase… and he turn not stray from the commandment"¹¹ [to include] even a prophet's commandment.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Devarim 17:20.
¹¹ Devarim 18:19. And now that you failed to obey the commandment of ‘ה to obey His word as delivered by His prophets, you will be held accountable for your actions.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:15:1)


**הַנִּמְצָאִים עִמּוֹ.** כִּי הָעָם נָפוֹצוּ מֵעָלָיו, וְאֵלּוּ נִשְׁתַּיְּרוּ:


**Who were [still] with him.** For the people had scattered from him, and these remained.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:18:1)


**גֵּי הַצְּבֹעִים.** שֶׁמְּצוּיִין בּוֹ הַצְּבוֹעִים, הוּא שֶׁשָּׁנִינוּ, רַבִּי מֵאִיר אוֹמֵר: אַף הַצָּבוּעַ. וְתִרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״אַפְעַיָּא״. וְכֵן פֵּרְשׁוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ ז״ל (בבא קמא טז א): צָבוּעַ זֶה אַפְעֶה, מִין שֶׁרֶץ רַע וּמַזִּיק:


**The Valley of the Tzvo'im.** Where the Tzvo'im are found. This is the animal referred to by R. Meir when he said, 'also the '.¹²צָּבוֹעַ Targum Yonoson renders, ''¹³אֲפָעַיָא and so did our Rabbis of sainted memory, explain, צָבוֹעַ is ,¹⁴אֶפְעֶה a species of fierce and destructive animals.

footnotes:
¹² Maseches Bava Kama 16a.
¹³ Perhaps it is the hyena or the fox.
¹⁴ A particular species of snake.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:19:1)


**וְחָרָשׁ לֹא יִמָּצֵא.** לֹא הָיָה מָצוּי בִּתְחִלַּת מַלְכוּת שָׁאוּל, כִּי הַפְּלִשְׁתִּים הַמּוֹשְׁלִים בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל, הֶעֱבִירוּ כָּל חָרָשֵׁי בַּרְזֶל מִיִּשְׂרָאֵל, פֶּן יַעֲשׂוּ כְּלֵי זַיִן לְהִלָּחֵם:


**A blacksmith was not to be found.** I.e., was not available at the beginning of Shaul's reign, because the Philistines who ruled over Yisroel, eliminated all the iron smiths from Yisroel, lest they make weapons to wage war.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:1)


**וַיֵּרְדוּ כָל יִשְׂרָאֵל הַפְּלִשְׁתִּים.** וּכְשֶׁהָיוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל צְרִיכִין לְחָרָשֵׁי בַּרְזֶל לִלְטוֹשׁ אִתִּים וְקַרְדֻּמּוֹת וּמַחֲרֵשׁוֹת, הָיוּ זְקוּקִים לֵירֵד אֶל אֶרֶץ פְּלִשְׁתִּים:


**And all of Yisroel would go down to the Philistines.** And when Yiroel needed iron smiths to sharpen mattocks, axes and tools, they had to go down to the land of the Philistines.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:2)


**מַחֲרַשְׁתּוֹ.** שו״ק בלע״ז:


**His plowshare.** *Soc* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:3)


**אֵתוֹ.** קולטר״א בלע״ז:


**His mattock.** *Coltre* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:20:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:4)


**קַרְדֻּמּוֹ.** בשגו״ר בלע״ז:


**His axe.** *Besague* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:20:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:20:5)


**מַחֲרַשְׁתּוֹ.** פישוייר״א בלע״ז:


**His tools.** *Fosoir* in O.F.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:21:1)


**וְהָיְתָה הַפְּצִירָה פִים לַמַּחֲרֵשׁוֹת.** וְהָיְתָה לָהֶם לְאוֹתָם שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם טוֹרַח לָרֶדֶת אֶל הַפְּלִשְׁתִּים לִלְטוֹשׁ, הָיְתָה הַפְּצִירָה פִים לימ״א בלע״ז, שֶׁיֵּשׁ לָהּ הַפְצַר פִּיּוֹת, כְּלוֹמַר פִּיּוֹת וְחִדּוּדִים הַרְבֵּה, הָיְתָה לָהֶם לְחַדֵּד הַמַּחֲרֶשֶׁת וְהָאִתִּים:


**There was a multi-edged file that was used for the tools.** For those who found it troublesome to go down to the Philistines to sharpen, had the פְּצִירָה פִים, *lime* in O.F., which has many mouths, i.e. many sharp and jagged surfaces to sharpen the tools and mattocks.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:21:2)


**וּלְשָׁלֹשׁ קִלְּשׁוֹן.** מַזְלֵג עָשׂוּי כְּמִין עָתֵר, שֶׁקּוֹרִין פורקד״א, וְלוֹ שָׁלוֹשׁ שִׁנִּים:


**Three pronged pitchforks.** A fork similar to a pitchfork, which is called *'forche'* [in O.F.], having three prongs.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:21:3)


**וּלְהַצִּיב הַדָּרְבָן.** לְחַדְּדוֹ בְּרֹאשׁוֹ לְנוֹעֲצוֹ וּלְהַצִּיבוֹ בְּתוֹךְ הַמַּרְדֵּעַ:


**And for setting the yoke.** To sharpen the tip, and to insert it and set it into the handle.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:22:1)


**וַתִּמָּצֵא לְשָׁאוּל.** עַל יְדֵי נֵס:


**But they could be found with Shaul.** By means of a miracle.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ See Vayikra Rabbah 25:8.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:23:1)


**מַצַּב פְּלִשְׁתִּים.** דֶּרֶךְ אַנְשֵׁי צָבָא לַעֲשׂוֹת מֵהֶם מַצָּב וּמַשְׁחִית, הֵם הַפּוֹשְׁטִים וְרָצִים אַחַר הַשָּׁלָל אֶל הָעִיר, שֶׁקּוֹרִין צנבי״ל בלע״ז:


**A Philistine garrison.** It is the custom of soldiers to form a garrison and a raiding party, [the latter] spread out into a city and seek its plunder. It is called *'cenbel'* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:23:2)


**מַצָּב.** הֵם נְצִיבִים לִשְׁמֹר שֶׁלֹּא יֵצְאוּ מִן הָעִיר מַשְׁחִיתִים בָּהֶם:


**Garrison.** They are the ones who stand guard that no raiders come out of the city against them.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 13:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/13:23:3)


**אֶל מַעֲבַר מִכְמָשׂ.** הַפְּלִשְׁתִּים הָיוּ חוֹנִים בְּמִכְמָשׂ, וּמִכְמָשׂ בָּהָר, וְיִשְׂרָאֵל בְּעִיר שֶׁשְּׁמָהּ גֶּבַע וְגֶבַע הָיְתָה בְּרֹאשׁ הָהָר, וּשְׁנֵי הָרִים הָיוּ זֶה כְּנֶגֶד זֶה, וְהַגַּיְא בֵּינֵיהֶם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמוּר בָּעִנְיָן. וּמַצַּב פְּלִשְׁתִּים נִגַּשׁ אֶל עֵבֶר מִכְמָשׂ שֶׁלְּצַד גֶּבַע, אֶל הַגַּיְא שֶׁבֵּינֵיהֶם:


**Towards the Pass of Michmos.** The Philistines were camped in Michmos, and Michmos was on a mountain. Yisroel was in a city named Geva, and Geva was also on top of a mountain, and those two mountains were opposite each other. The valley was between them, as is stated in this episode, "and the Phillistine garrison went out toward the Pass of Michmos," which is on the side toward Geva, to the valley between them.



## Chapter 14


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:1:1)


**מֵעֵבֶר הַלָּז.** כָּל ״הַלָּז״ וְ״לָזֶה״ שֶׁבַּמִּקְרָא אֵינוֹ לְשׁוֹן ״הַזֶּה״, אֶלָּא לְשׁוֹן דָּבָר שֶׁכְּנֶגְדוֹ, וְהוּא מַרְאֵהוּ בְּאֶצְבַּע, וּבְלַעַ״ז אֵינוֹ לְשׁוֹן ציש״ט בְּלַעַ״ז, אֶלָּא ציי״ל בְּלַעַ״ז:


**On the other side.** Every הַלָּז and לָזֶה in Scripture, is not an expression of הַזֶּה [this], but it is an expression of opposite [the speaker], and he is pointing it out with his finger. It is not an expression of *'cest'* in O.F., but of *'cel'* in O.F.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:1:2)


**מֵעֵבֶר הַלָּז.** גֵי שְׁנֵי הֶהָרִים:


**On the other side.** Of the valley [between] the two mountains.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:2:1)


**בִּקְצֵה הַגִּבְעָה.** בְּסוֹף הַגִּבְעָה:


**At the edge of Givah.** At the end of Givah.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:3:1)


**נֹשֵׂא אֵפוֹד.** אוּרִים וְתֻמִּים:


**Wore the Eiphod.** Meaning the Urim and Tumim.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:4:1)


**וּבֵין הַמַּעְבְּרוֹת אֲשֶׁר בִּקֵּשׁ יוֹנָתָן לַעֲבֹר.** כֵּן הָיָה עָשׂוּי, שֵׁן הַסֶּלַע הָיָה מֵהָעֵבֶר מִזֶּה לְבֵין הַמַּעְבְּרוֹת מִזֶּה, וְשֵׁן הַסֶּלַע לְבֵין הַמַּעְבְּרוֹת מִזֶּה הַגַּיְא הוּא קָרוּי בֵּין הַמַּעְבְּרוֹת, וְהָיָה לוֹ סֶלַע מִכָּאן, וְסֶלַע מִכָּאן, זֶה בְּעֶבְרוֹ מִזֶּה, וְזֶה בְּעֶבְרוֹ מִזֶּה, וְהַגַּיְא בֵּין שְׁנֵי הָעֲבָרִים, אוֹתוֹ הַגַּיְא בִּקֵּשׁ יוֹנָתָן לַעֲבֹר:


**Between the passes that Yonoson wanted to cross.** It was thus made: There was a rocky precipice from this side of 'between the passes,' and there was a rocky precipice from the other side of 'between the passes.' The valley is called 'between the passes,' and it had a stone from here, and a stone from here, one on this side and one on the other side, and the valley was between the two passes. It was that valley that Yonoson wanted to cross.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:4:2)


**שֵׁן הַסֶּלַע.** שֵׁם שֵׁן הָאֶחָד ׳בּוֹצֵץ׳, וְשֵׁם הַשֵּׁנִי ׳סֶנֶה׳:


**Rocky precipice.** The name of one precipice was Botzeitz and the name of the second was Seneh.¹

footnotes:
¹ The names of the two rocky crags are indicative of their terrain. Botzeitz as implied by its name, had a smooth and slippery surface making it very difficult to scale. Seneh had a “treaded on” surface.—Radak



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:5:1)


**מָצוּק מִצָּפוֹן.** מְשֻׁפָּע וְעוֹלֶה מִצַּד צָפוֹן אֶל מִכְמָשׂ:


**Jutted out from the north.** Slanting from the north side toward Michmos.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 14:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/14:5:2)
