³ The New Moon [=Rosh Chodesh] was celebrated as a festive day. See II Melachim 4:23 and Hosheia 2:13.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:5:2)


**וְאָנֹכִי יָשֹׁב אֵשֵׁב עִם הַמֶּלֶךְ לֶאֱכֹל.** לְשׁוֹן הֹוֶה הוּא, וְאָנֹכִי רָגִיל לֵישֵׁב עִם הַמֶּלֶךְ תָּמִיד לֶאֱכֹל סָמוּךְ לוֹ:


**And I usually sit with the king to eat.** This is the present tense [meaning] 'and I am accustomed to sit with the king regularly, to eat beside him.'


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:5:3)


**וְשִׁלַּחְתָּנִי.** מֵעַתָּה, וְנִסְתַּרְתִּי עַד הָעֶרֶב שֶׁל יוֹם שְׁלִישִׁי, וְלֹא אֶהְיֶה עִם הָאוֹכְלִים, וְיִפָּקֵד מוֹשָׁבִי, וְיִתְמַהּ הַמֶּלֶךְ עָלַי, אוֹ מָחָר אוֹ לְמָחָרָתוֹ:


**Let me leave.** From now, and I will hide until the evening of the third day, and I will not be with the people eating, and my seat will be empty; the king will wonder about me either tomorrow or the following day.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:6:1)


**נִשְׁאֹל נִשְׁאַל.** רְשׁוּת שָׁאַל מִמֶּנִּי:


**Asked.** He asked me for permission.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:6:2)


**זֶבַח הַיָּמִים.** מִשָּׁנָה לְשָׁנָה בַּזְּמַן הַזֶּה:


**An annual feast-offering.** From year to year at this time.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ It was customary for family members to offer a yearly sacrifice. Elkonoh, too, used to go to Shiloh every year [See above 1:21].



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:1)


**אִם כֹּה יֹאמַר טוֹב.** שֶׁלֹּא יֶחֱרֶה לוֹ:


**If he says thus, 'Good'.** I.e., he is not angry.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ It is a sign that he harbors no evil.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:2)


**שָׁלוֹם לְעַבְדֶּךָ.** אֵין בְּלִבּוֹ לְהָרְגֵנִי:


**It is well for your servant.** He has no intention to kill me.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:3)


**וְאִם חָרֹה יֶחֱרֶה.** זֶהוּ שֶׁאֱהֵא לוֹ מָצוּי לְהָרְגֵנִי:


**But if he gets very angry.** This is because he wants me to be accessible so that he can kill me.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:7:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:7:4)


**כָלְתָה הָרָעָה מֵעִמּוֹ.** סוֹף דָּבָר מַחֲשָׁבָה רָעָה לוֹ עָלַי:


**He has decided upon evil.** The conclusion is, that he has an evil thought concerning me.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:8:1)


**וְעָשִׂיתָ חֶסֶד.** לְשַׁלְּחֵנִי:


**Be kind. To let me go.⁶**

footnotes:
⁶ Or by notifying me of what is happening.—Metzudas Dovid.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:9:1)


**חָלִילָה לָּךְ.** כָּזֹאת לְחָשְׁדֵנִי שֶׁאֵדַע בּוֹ שֶׁכָּלְתָה מֵעִמּוֹ הָרָעָה וְלֹא אַגִּיד לָךְ:


**Far be it from you.** To suspect me thus, that I know he has decided upon evil, and not to tell you.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:10:1)


**מִי יַגִּיד לִי.** בִּמְקוֹם שֶׁאֶסָּתֵר שָׁם:


**Who will tell me.** In the place where I will hide.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:12:1)


**ה׳ אֱלֹהֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** לְשׁוֹן שְׁבוּעָה:


**Adonoy the God of Yisroel.** This is an expression of oath.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:12:2)


**הַשְּׁלִשִׁית.** בִּימֵי מָחָר, זֶהוּ יוֹם שְׁלִישִׁי כְּשָׁעָה שֶׁל עַכְשָׁו, כִּי מָחָר חֹדֶשׁ, וְשֶׁמָּא לֹא יִתְמַהּ מָחָר אָבִי, אֲבָל יִתְמַהּ בַּיּוֹם הַשְּׁלִישִׁי:


**On the third day.** Of the following day. That means the third day at this time, for tomorrow is the new moon, and tomorrow my father might not wonder; but he will wonder on the third day.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:13:1)


**כֹּה יַעֲשֶׂה לִּי.** הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, כִּי אֶחְקֹר אֶת אָבִי וְאֶמְצָא שֶׁהוּא טוֹב אֵלֶיךָ, אִם לֹא אֶשְׁלַח עַל יְדֵי שָׁלִיחַ וְגָלִיתִי אָזְנֶךָ, כִּי הַטּוֹבָה אֲנִי יָכוֹל לְגַלּוֹת, וְאִם יֵיטִב אֶל אָבִי לְהָרַע לָךְ, אוֹתָהּ לֹא אֲגַלֶּה, פֶּן יִוָּדַע לְאָבִי מָקוֹם שֶׁאַתָּה שָׁם, וְגָלִיתִי אֲנִי בְּעַצְמִי אֶת אָזְנֶךָ:


**So may Adonoy do to Yonoson.** The Holy One, Blessed is He, when I question my father, and find that he is favorable toward you, if I do not [send word] through a messenger and reveal to you, for good I am able to reveal. But if it pleases my father to do evil to you, that I will not reveal [by messenger], lest my father find out your whereabouts,⁷ but I will personally reveal it to you.

footnotes:
⁷ I.e., the messenger might later reveal Dovid’s whereabouts to Shaul.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:14:1)


**וְלֹא אִם עוֹדֶנִּי חָי.** לְשׁוֹן בַּקָּשָׁה הוּא, כְּמוֹ (מלכים ב ה:יז): ״וְלֹא יֻתַּן נָא לְעַבְדֶּךָ״. וְאֵינוֹ זָז מִמַּשְׁמָעוֹ, וְלֹא תַּעֲשֶׂה לִי טוֹבָה בְּעוֹדֶנִּי חָי, שֶׁתַּעֲשֶׂה עִמִּי חֶסֶד ה׳ בְּטֶרֶם אָמוּת, וּמַהוּ הַחֶסֶד, שֶׁלֹּא תַכְרִית אֶת חַסְדְּךָ מֵעִם בֵּיתִי, בְּזֹאת תִּכְרֹת לִי בְּרִית בְּחַיָּי:


**I will not [ask of you] if I still be alive.** An expression of request, as in and if only [וָלאׁ] it will be given to your bondsman.'⁸ It is not removed from its usual meaning, 'and will you not do with me good while I am still alive, and do with me the kindness of Adonoy before I die?' And what is the kindness? That you will not discontinue your kindness with my household. Regarding this, you will make a covenant with me in my lifetime.

footnotes:
⁸ II Melachim 5:17.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:15:1)


**וְלֹא בְּהַכְרִית ה׳.** וְגַם לֹא תָּסִיר חַסְדְּךָ מֵה׳, כִּי אַף בְּבֹא הַפֻּרְעָנוּת עַל בֵּית אָבִי, שֶׁיָּדַעְתִּי שֶׁיַּכְרִית הַמָּקוֹם אֶת אוֹיְבֶיךָ:


**Not even when Adonoy cuts off.** And also, you will not remove your kindness from them even when retribution befalls my father's house, for I know that God will cut off your enemies.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ And being that my father’s family is included among your enemies, you will not kill them.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:16:1)


**וּבִקֵּשׁ ה׳.** אֶת הַבְּרִית הַזֹּאת:


**May Adonoy exact punishment.** Of this covenant.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:16:2)


**מִיַּד אֹיְבֵי דָוִד.** ״כִּנָּה הַכָּתוּב, וּפֵרוּשׁוֹ: בִּקֵּשׁ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא אֶת הָעָוֹן מִיַּד דָּוִד, כְּשֶׁעָבַר עַל הַבְּרִית וְאָמַר לִמְפִיבֹשֶׁת (שמואל ב יט:ל): ״אַתָּה וְצִיבָא תַּחְלְקוּ אֶת הַשָּׂדֶה״, יָצְתָה בַּת קוֹל וְאָמְרָה: רְחַבְעָם וְיָרָבְעָם יַחְלְקוּ אֶת הַמַּלְכוּת. וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וְאִתְפְּרַע לֵיהּ ה׳ מִיַּד שָׂנְאֵי דָוִד:


**From Dovid's enemies.** Scripture euphemized. Its real meaning is that the The Holy One, Blessed is He, demanded the punishment of the transgression from Dovid.¹⁰ When he transgressed the covenant, and said to Mefiboshes, "You and Tziva shall divide the field,"¹¹ a *bas kol* came forth, and said, 'Rechavam and Yorovom shall divide the kingdom.'¹² And so did Targum Yonoson render, 'and Adonoy took retribution from Dovid's enemies.'

footnotes:
¹⁰ Others maintain that ‘Dovid’s enemies’ refers to Shaul and Avneir.—Mahri Kra.
¹¹ II Shmuel 19:30.
¹² See Maseches Shabbos 56b and Maseches Yoma 22b.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:1)


**מָחָר חֹדֶשׁ.** וְדֶרֶךְ כָּל אוֹכְלֵי שֻׁלְחַן הַמֶּלֶךְ לָבֹא בְּיוֹם מוֹעֵד אֶל הַשֻּׁלְחָן:


**Tomorrow is the New Moon.** And it is customary for all those who eat at the king's table to come on the festive day to the table.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:2)


**וְנִפְקַדְתָּ.** אָבִי יִפְקְדְךָ וְיִשְׁאַל הֵיכָן אַתָּה. **כִּי יִפָּקֵד מוֹשָׁבֶךָ.** ״שֶׁיִּהְיֶה מוֹשָׁבְךָ חָסֵר, שֶׁאַתָּה יוֹשֵׁב בּוֹ וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וְתִתְבְּעֵי אֲרֵי יְהֵי מְרוֹחַ בֵּית אִסְחָרוּתָךְ:


**And you will be missed.** My father will miss you, and ask where you are. Because your seat will be empty.¹³ For your seat in which you sit, will be empty, and so did Targum Yonoson render, 'and you will be sought, for your seat will be vacant.'

footnotes:
¹³ Yonoson assumed that out of respect for Dovid no one would occupy his seat.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:3)


**וְנִפְקַדְתָּ.** לְשׁוֹן זִכָּרוֹן:


**And you will be missed.** An expression of remembering.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:18:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:18:4)


**כִּי יִפָּקֵד.** לְשׁוֹן חִסָּרוֹן:


**Will be empty.** An expression of missing.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:19:1)


**וְשִׁלַּשְׁתָּ.** שְׁלֹשֶׁת יָמִים, וְאָז תֵּרֵד מְאֹד, כְּלוֹמַר לִכְשֶׁתַּגִּיעַ הַיּוֹם הַשְּׁלִישִׁי, תֵּרֵד בִּמְקוֹם סֵתֶר וְתִתְחַבֵּא הַרְבֵּה, כִּי אָז יְבַקְּשׁוּךָ, וּבָאתָ אֶל הַמָּקוֹם הַסֵּתֶר הַזֶּה אֲשֶׁר אַתָּה נִסְתָּר בּוֹ הַיּוֹם, שֶׁהוּא יוֹם מַעֲשֵׂה מְלָאכָה וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״בְּיוֹמָא דְחוֹלָא״, שֶׁאוֹתוֹ הַיּוֹם נִסְתָּר, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (פסוק כד): ״וַיִּסָּתֵר דָּוִד בַּשָּׂדֶה״ מִיָּד, ״וַיְהִי הַחֹדֶשׁ״ מָחָר:


**For three days.** You shall triple the days, and then you should go down very much, i.e., when the third day arrives,¹⁴ you should go down into a secret place, and hide very well, because they will seek you then. And you shall come to this secret place, where you are hiding today, which is a work day.¹⁵ And similarly Targum Yonoson renders, on a week day, for he hid on that day, as it is stated, 'and Dovid hid in the field,'¹⁶ and immediately, 'it was the New Moon'¹⁷ on the next day.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Others translate וְשִׁלַשְׁתָּ as “mark three times” [the spot where to hide] to avoid any misunderstanding. The same expression [וְשִׁלַשְׁתָּ], is used to tell the Bnei Yisroel to mark the borders of the land into three parts in Devarim 19:3.
¹⁵ I.e., the day after Rosh Chodesh is described as a work day in comparison to Rosh Chodesh which is considered a holiday because they did not engage in their usual work.
¹⁶ Below verse 24.
¹⁷ Ibid.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:19:2)


**הָאֶבֶן הָאָזֶל.** אֶבֶן שֶׁהָיְתָה אוֹת לְהוֹלְכֵי דְרָכִים:


**The marker stone.** A stone which was a landmark for travelers.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ הׇאָזֶל meaning “[a stone for] those who go.” Others, maintain that the marker stone was used as a target at which arrows were shot.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:19:3)


**הָאָזֶל.** הוֹלְכֵי הַדֶּרֶךְ, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״אֶבֶן אָתָא״, אֶבֶן הָאוֹת:


**The marker** for those who travel. And similarly Targum Yonoson renders אֶבֶן אָתָא, 'the stone [that served as] a sign.'¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁹ For travelers.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:20:1)


**צִדָּה אוֹרֶה.** לֹא מַפִּיק ה״א, וּפִתְרוֹן צִדָּה כְּמוֹ לְצַד, כָּל תֵּיבָה שֶׁצְּרִיכָה לָמֶ״ד בִּתְחִלָּתָהּ, הֵטִיל לָהּ ה״א בְּסוֹפָהּ, בְּצַד אוֹתָהּ אֶבֶן, אוֹרֶה חִצִּים לְמַטָּרָה, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יָבִין הַנַּעַר, וְזֶה סִימָן יִהְיֶה לְךָ לְנַחֵשׁ אִם אַתָּה צָרִיךְ לִבְרֹחַ:


**I will shoot in that direction.** This is not a dotted ה׳. Thus צִדָּה is interpreted like לַצַּד [to a side].²⁰ Every word which requires a 'ל' as a prefix, Scripture puts a 'ה' as a suffix,²¹ meaning, 'at the side of that stone, I will shoot arrows to a target' so that the youth will not understand, and this sign will be for you to divine whether you need to run away.

footnotes:
²⁰ Radak explains צִדָּה as if it had a ׳מַפִּיק ה׳ meaning ‘to its side, i.e., the side of the stone’ he will shoot the arrow.
²¹ Maseches Yevamos 13b.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:21:1)


**וְהִנֵּה אֶשְׁלַח וְגוֹ׳.** וְדֶרֶךְ הַמְבַקֵּשׁ חֵץ הַיָּרוּי, הוֹלֵךְ עַד מָקוֹם שֶׁרוֹאֶה שֶׁהַחֵץ הוֹלֵךְ, וְאֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לְכַוֵּן יָפֶה, פְּעָמִים שֶׁהוּא מְחַפְּשָׂהּ וְהַחֵץ לְהַלָּן מִמֶּנּוּ, וּפְעָמִים שֶׁהוּא הוֹלֵךְ לְהַלָּן מִן הַחֵץ וּמְחַפְּשָׂהּ, וְהַנִּחוּשׁ הַזֶּה יִהְיֶה לָךְ:


**Behold, I will then send…** And it is customary for the one who is looking for a shot arrow to go to the place where he sees the arrow flying, but he cannot calculate exactly; sometimes he searches for it, and the arrow is beyond him, and sometimes he goes beyond the arrow and searches for it, and you shall divine with this [sign].


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:21:2)


**אִם אָמֹר אֹמַר לַנַּעַר וְגוֹ׳ קָחֶנּוּ וָבֹאָה.** צֵא אַתָּה בְּעַצְמְךָ מִמְּקוֹם מַחֲבוֹאֲךָ, וְקָחֶנּוּ וּבֹא אֵלַי, כִּי אֵין לְךָ לִירָא, כִּי שָׁלוֹם לָךְ, הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא חָפֵץ שֶׁתְּהֵא כָּאן וְלֹא תִירָא, וַאֲפִלּוּ שָׁמַעְתִּי מֵאַבָּא רָעָה:


**If I say to the lad…take [the arrows] and come.²²** You should emerge from your hiding place, and take it, and come to me, for you have nothing to fear, for it is well with you. The Holy One, Blessed is He, desires that you be here, and do not fear, even if I have heard evil from [my] father.²³

footnotes:
²² To signal Dovid’s acceptability by Shaul.
²³ It is apparent that Rashi holds that Yonoson used the shooting of the arrows as a form of divination to determine whether ה׳ wanted Dovid to stay or to flee. According to Rashi, Yonoson did not plan to shoot the arrows in accordance with Shaul’s attitude toward Dovid, i.e., he would shoot the arrows close by as a signal for Dovid to come back if Shaul’s answer was positive towards Dovid; and he would shoot the arrows far off to indicate for Dovid to escape if Shaul’s answer was unfavorable to Dovid.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:22:1)


**וְאִם כֹּה אוֹמַר וְגוֹ׳ לֵךְ כִּי שִׁלַּחֲךָ.** הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא אוֹמֵר לְךָ לִבְרֹחַ וּלְהִמָּלֵט:


**But, if I say thus…then go for Adonoy has sent you away.** The Holy One, Blessed is He, tells you to run away and escape.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:23:1)


**וְהַדָּבָר אֲשֶׁר דִּבַּרְנוּ.** בְּרִית שֶׁכָּרַתְנוּ יַחַד:


**But as for the matter of which we have spoken.** The covenant which we made together.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:23:2)


**הִנֵּה ה׳ בֵּינִי וּבֵינֶךָ.** עֵד עַל אוֹתוֹ דָבָר:


**Behold, Adonoy is [witness] between me and you.** A witness concerning that matter.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:25:1)


**אֶל מוֹשַׁב הַקִּיר.** בְּרֹאשׁ הַמִּטָּה אֵצֶל הַקִּיר:


**On the seat against the wall.** At the head of the couch, next to the wall.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:25:2)


**וַיָּקָם יְהוֹנָתָן.** עָמַד מִמְּקוֹמוֹ, לְפִי שֶׁאֵין דֶּרֶךְ הַבֵּן לִהְיוֹת מֵיסֵב אֵצֶל אָבִיו, שֶׁדַּרְכָּן הָיָה לֶאֱכֹל מְסֻבִּין עַל הַמִּטּוֹת, וְהָיָה דָוִד מֵיסֵב בֵּין יְהוֹנָתָן וּבֵין שָׁאוּל, עַכְשָׁו שֶׁלֹּא בָא דָוִד, לֹא הֵסֵב יְהוֹנָתָן, עַד שֶׁיָּשַׁב אַבְנֵר מִצַּד שָׁאוּל, וְאַחַר כָּךְ יָשַׁב יְהוֹנָתָן מִצַּד אַבְנֵר וְאִם תֹּאמַר לֹא יָשַׁב כְּלָל, הֲרֵי הוּא אוֹמֵר (פסוק לד): וַיָּקָם יְהוֹנָתָן מֵעַל הַשֻּׁלְחָן, מִכְּלָל שֶׁיָּשַׁב:


**Yonoson rose.** He stood up from his place, because it is not proper for a son to recline beside his father;²⁴ their custom was to eat while reclining on couches, and Dovid would recline between Yonoson and Shaul. Now that Dovid did not come, Yonoson did not recline until Avneir sat down beside Shaul, and afterwards, Yonoson sat beside Avneir. And if you say that he did not sit as all, Scripture states, 'And Yonoson rose from the table,'²⁵ implying that he had been sitting.

footnotes:
²⁴ Others suggest that Yonoson wanted to keep his distance from Shaul and his show of respect was only a guise.
²⁵ Below verse 34.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:26:1)


**מִקְרֶה הוּא.** קֶרִי רָאָה:


**Something happened.** He has experienced [a discharge of] semen.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:26:2)


**בִּלְתִּי טָהוֹר הוּא.** וַעֲדַיִן לֹא טָבַל לְקִרְיוֹ, שֶׁאִלּוּ טָבַל לְקִרְיוֹ, אֵין צָרִיךְ הַעֲרֵב שֶׁמֶשׁ לְחֻלִּין:


**He must be impure.** And he has not yet immersed himself for his [discharge of] semen, for had he immersed himself for his [discharge of] semen, he would not have to wait for sunset [to eat] ordinary food.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ See Vayikra 22:4.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:26:3)


**כִּי לֹא טָהוֹר.** זוֹ הִיא נְתִינַת טַעַם לַדָּבָר, לְפִי שֶׁאֵינוֹ טָהוֹר, לְפִיכָךְ לֹא בָא, שֶׁלֹּא יְטַמֵּא אֶת הַסְּעוּדָה:


**For he is not ritually pure.** This [clause] gives reason for the matter, because he is not pure, he, therefore, did not come, so that he would not contaminate the feast.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ Either because they were accustomed to maintain the stringencies associated with ritual purity even for ordinary food; or perhaps the New Moon feast consisted of the consecrated food of the peace offerings.—Radak.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:27:1)


**מִמָּחֳרַת הַחֹדֶשׁ.** מִמָּחֳרַת חִדּוּשׁ הַלְּבָנָה:


**The day after the New Moon.** The day following the renewal²⁸ of the moon.

footnotes:
²⁸ חֹדֶשׁ here means ‘renewal’ i.e., Rosh Chodesh and not ‘month.’


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:27:2)


**הַשֵּׁנִי.** בְּיוֹם שֵׁנִי לַחֹדֶשׁ:


**The second [day].** The second day of the month.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:29:1)


**וְהוּא צִוָּה לִי אָחִי.** גְּדוֹל הַבַּיִת צִוָּה לִי שֶׁאֶהְיֶה שָׁם, וְהוּא אָחִי אֱלִיאָב:


**And he, my brother, summoned me.** The eldest of the household, summoned me that I be here;²⁹ and he is my brother Eliav.

footnotes:
²⁹ The phrase נִשְׁאֹל נִשְׁאַל reflects the two reasons for his request to go to Beis Lechem. One was to participate in his family’s annual sacrifice and the other reason was because the eldest of the household had summoned him there.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:29:2)


**אִמָּלְטָה.** אשקמוצי״ד בלע״ז, אֵלֵךְ יוֹם אֶחָד וְאָבוֹא:


**Let me hurry away.** *Esmucier* in O.F. I shall go away for a day and come [back].



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:30:1)


**נַעֲוַת הַמַּרְדּוּת.** לְשׁוֹן נָע, אִשָּׁה נָעָה וְנָדָה, יוֹצְאָנִית, כַּאֲשֶׁר תֹּאמַר ׳זַעֲוָה׳ מִן ׳זָע׳, כֵּן תֹּאמַר ׳נַעֲוָה׳ מִן ׳נָע׳, וְהַתָּי״ו מִן הַדִּבּוּק, שֶׁהוּא דָבוּק לְמַרְדּוּת:


**A straying woman, deserving of punishment.**נָע is an expression of straying and wandering, a gadding woman. Just as you have זַעֲוָה from זָע, similarly you have נַעֲוָה from נָע, and the 'ת' is for construct state, for it is connected to הַמַּרְדּוּת.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:30:2)


**הַמַּרְדּוּת.** שֶׁהִיא רְאוּיָה לִרְדּוֹת וּלְיַסֵּר. דָּבָר אַחֵר: כְּשֶׁחָטְפוּ בְּנֵי בִּנְיָמִן מִבְּנוֹת שִׁילֹה שֶׁיָּצְאוּ לָחוּל בַּכְּרָמִים, הָיָה שָׁאוּל בַּיְשָׁן וְלֹא רָצָה לַחֲטֹף, עַד שֶׁבָּאתָה הִיא עַצְמָהּ וְהֵעִיזָה פָנֶיהָ וְרָדְפָה אַחֲרָיו:


**Deserving of punishment.** Who deserves to be chastised and disciplined. Another explanation is that when the men of Binyomin grabbed the girls of Shiloh, who came out to dance in the vineyards,³⁰ Shaul was bashful, and did not want to grab, until she herself came, behaving insolently, and pursued him.

footnotes:
³⁰ See Shoftim 21:20-21.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:30:3)


**נַעֲוַת.** עַל שֵׁם הַכְּרָמִים, וְהִיא גַּת, כְּמוֹ (עבודה זרה עד ב): נַעֲוָה אַרְתְּחוּ. ״יִטּוּפוּן נַעֲווֹהִי בַּחֲמַר״ (תרגום פרשת ויחי פסוק חכלילי עינים (בראשית מט:יב)), וְלֹא מִן הַשֵּׁם:


**Straying.** Because of the vineyards. And that is the winepress, as in 'purge the winepress'³¹ [and] 'His winepresses will drip with wine,'³² in the Targum's translation of 'His eyes are red [from wine].'

footnotes:
³¹ Maseches Avodah Zorah 74b.
³² Bereishis 49:12 Targum Unkelus.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:34:1)


**נֶעְצַב אֶל דָּוִד.** בִּשְׁבִיל דָּוִד:


**He was saddened over Dovid.** Concerning Dovid.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:34:2)


**כִּי הִכְלִימוֹ אָבִיו.** בִּשְׁבִיל דָּוִד:


**Because his father had humiliated him.** Concerning Dovid.³³

footnotes:
³³ Yonoson did not eat any food on the second day of the New Moon for two reasons and both were ‘concerning Dovid.’ Firstly, he was “saddened” that his father was set upon killing Dovid and secondly because his father “humiliated” him both by verbal abuse and by action [throwing the spear].—Radak



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:35:1)


**לְמוֹעֵד דָּוִד.** לַמּוֹעֵד אֲשֶׁר קָבַע לוֹ דָּוִד:


**For the meeting with Dovid.** At the time which Dovid had set for him.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:36:1)


**לְהַעֲבִירוֹ.** הַחֵצִי עָבַר אֶת הַנַּעַר:


**Beyond him.** The arrow went beyond the lad.



### Verse 41

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:41:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:41:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:41:1)


**מֵאֵצֶל הַנֶּגֶב.** (תרגום:) ״מִסְּטַר אֶבֶן אָתָא דִּלְקֳבֵל דָּרוֹמָא״:


**From near the southern side.** [Targum renders,] 'from the side of the landmark stone which was toward the south.'


###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:41:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:41:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:41:2)


**עַד דָּוִד הִגְדִּיל.** הִרְבָּה לִבְכּוֹת:


**Until Dovid exceeded.** I.e., he cried more.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ I.e., ‘more than Yonoson.’ However, according to Ralbag, ‘more than is fitting.’ Dovid cried more than Yonoson because unlike Yonoson who could return home now, Dovid would have to flee from Shaul who was seeking to kill him.



### Verse 42

###### Rashi on I Samuel 20:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/20:42:1)


**לֵךְ לְשָׁלוֹם.** וְהַשְּׁבוּעָה אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁבַּעְנוּ, ה׳ יִהְיֶה עֵד עָלֶיהָ עַד עוֹלָם:


**Go to peace.** And the oath which we have sworn, may ה׳ be its witness forever.



## Chapter 21


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:1)


**וְאֶת הַנְּעָרִים יוֹדַעְתִּי.** לְשׁוֹן טֹרַח, הַנְּעָרִים אֲשֶׁר יָצְאוּ עִמִּי, הִטְרַחְתִּים לָרוּץ לְפָנַי אֶל מָקוֹם פְּלוֹנִי אַלְמוֹנִי:


**And as for the youths, I have sent them ahead.** An expression of troubling; the young men who came out with me, I have troubled them to go before me to a secret, hidden place.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:2)


**יוֹדַעְתִּי.** כְּמוֹ ״וַיֹּדַע בָּהֶם אֶת אַנְשֵׁי סֻכּוֹת״ (שופטים ח:טז):


**I have troubled.** Similar to, 'And with them he broke [וַידַׁע] the men of Succos.'¹

footnotes:
¹ Shoftim 8:16.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:3)


**פְּלֹנִי אַלְמֹנִי.** ׳אַלְמוֹן׳ מֵאֵין שֵׁם, שֶׁאֵינִי חָפֵץ לְהַזְכִּיר, כִּי דְבַר סֵתֶר הוּא:


**Certain secret.** [אַלְמֹנִי is derived from] 'אַלְמוֹן' [=widower], bereft of a name; for I do not want to mention it, because it is a secret matter.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:3:4)


**פְּלֹנִי.** כְּמוֹ ״כִּי יִפָּלֵא״ (דברים יז:ח), יִתְכַּסֶּה:


**Hidden.** Similar to כִּי יִפָּלֵא [=will be concealed],² will be hidden.

footnotes:
² Devarim 17:8.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:4:1)


**אוֹ הַנִּמְצָא.** אִם אֵין חֲמִשָּׁה, תֵּן לִי מַה שֶּׁנִּמְצָא בְּיָדְךָ:


**Or whatever there is.** If there are not five,³ give me whatever is in your possession.

footnotes:
³ Talmud Yerushalmi Succah 5:8 explains why Dovid asked for five loaves of bread. Dovid asked for bread from Achimelech who was a member of the family of kohanim that had just completed its service; and their share was five loaves of the showbread.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:5:1)


**אַךְ מֵאִשָּׁה.** בְּקֹשִׁי אֲנִי מַאֲכִילָן לְזָרִים, אַךְ זֶה אִי אֶפְשָׁר שֶׁאַאֲכִילֶנּוּ לִטְמֵאִים:


**From women.** With difficulty, I give them to non-kohanim to eat, but it is impossible to give it to unclean people to eat.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:1)


**עֲצוּרָה לָנוּ.** עֲצוּרָה מִמֶּנּוּ:


**Have been kept from us.** Withheld from us.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ They seem to have treated even ordinary food as if it were holy food as in above 20:26.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:2)


**וַיִּהְיוּ כְלֵי הַנְּעָרִים קֹדֶשׁ.** טְהוֹרִים:


**The garments of the youths were pure.** [Literally 'holy'] pure.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:3)


**וְהוּא דֶּרֶךְ חֹל.** וְהַלֶּחֶם מִשֶּׁנִּסְתַּלֵּק מֵעַל הַשֻּׁלְחָן וְהִקְטִירוּ הַבָּזִיכִין, קָרוֹב הוּא לִהְיוֹת חֻלִּין, שֶׁיָּצָא מִידֵי מְעִילָה מִשֶּׁיִּהְיֶה לוֹ שְׁעַת הֶתֵּר לַכֹּהֲנִים:


**Even though it is an ordinary mission.** And the bread, once it has been removed from the table, and they have burned the spoonfuls [of frankincense], it is almost common, because it has been excluded from [the prohibition of] מְעִילָה as soon as it becomes permitted for the kohanim [to eat].⁵

footnotes:
⁵ The phrase וְהוּא דֶרֶךְ הֹל can then be translated as ‘it has already been made non-sacred’ referring to the showbreads [not to the mission], that they had lost some of their sanctity once the frankincense had been burnt.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:6:4)


**וְאַף כִּי הַיּוֹם יִקְדַּשׁ בַּכֶּלִי.** וַאֲפִלּוּ אִם הַיּוֹם הָיְתָה תְּחִלַּת קְדֻשָּׁתוֹ עַל הַשֻּׁלְחָן, הָיִיתִי זָקוּק לִטְּלוֹ מִשָּׁם וּלְהַאֲכִילֶנּוּ, שֶׁאֲחָזוֹ בּוּלְמוּס וּמְסֻכָּן הוּא, כָּךְ מְפֹרָשׁ בִּמְנָחוֹת:


**Even more so today it will remain sacred in the vessel.** Even if today were the beginning of its sanctity on the table, I would be obliged to take it from there and feed ourselves, for a ravenous hunger has seized us, and our lives are in danger. Thus it is explained in [Maseches] Menachos.⁶



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:7:1)


**לָשׂוּם לֶחֶם חֹם.** אַחֵר, בְּיוֹם הִלָּקַח זֶה וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (יומא כא א): חֹם בְּיוֹם הִלָּקְחוֹ, גַּם בְּיוֹם סִלּוּקוֹ, חֹם כְּיוֹם סִדּוּרוֹ:


**In order to put hot bread.** Other warm bread on the day this was removed. Our Rabbis said, that it was warm on the day it was taken, i.e., it was as warm on the day it was removed as it was on the day it was arranged.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ See Maseches Menachos 96a and Chagigah 26b which describes the miracle of the showbread; it retained its warmth and freshness after lying on the table more than a week. The preservation of warmth and freshness symbolized that ה׳ preserves the strength of the Bnei Yisroel and never allows it to fully dissipate.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:8:1)


**נֶעְצָר לִפְנֵי ה׳.** עוֹצֵר עַצְמוֹ לִפְנֵי אֹהֶל מוֹעֵד, לַעֲסֹק בַּתּוֹרָה:


**Who remained before Adonoy.** He tarried before the Tent of Meeting to engage in the study of Torah.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:8:2)


**אֲבִיר הָרֹעִים.** אַב בֵּית דִּין:


**Chief of Shaul's shepherds.** The head of the tribunal.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:9:1)


**וְאֵין יֵשׁ פֹּה.** וְאִם יֵשׁ פֹּה:


**Perhaps you have in your possession.** And if there is here.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:9:2)


**נָחוּץ.** בִּבְהִילוּת:


**Urgent.** Hurried.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:10:1)


**לוּטָה.** כְּרוּכָה, וְכֵן ״וַיָּלֶט פָּנָיו בְּאַדַּרְתּוֹ״ (מלכים א יט:יג):


**Wrapped.** It was wrapped, and similarly, 'and he wrapped [וַיָלֶט] his face in his mantle.'⁸

footnotes:
⁸ I Melachim 19:13.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:10:2)


**אַחֲרֵי הָאֵפוֹד.** ״אַחַר שֶׁשָּׁאֲלוּ בָּאוּרִים וְתֻמִּים, אָמַר לוֹ כֵּן וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״בָּתַר דְּשָׁאִיל לֵיהּ בְּאֵפוֹדָא:


**Behind the *eiphod.*** After having consulted the Urim and Tumim he said this [statement]. And so did Targum Yonoson render, 'after he had consulted the eiphod for him.'⁹

footnotes:
⁹ I.e., אַחֲרֵי הָאֵפוֹד is not part of Achimelech’s reply to Dovid, rather it relates to the sequence of events. Only after asking the Urim and Tumim did Achimelech tell Dovid to take the sword.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:12:1)


**הֲלֹא זֶה דָוִד מֶלֶךְ הָאָרֶץ.** אָמְרוּ לוֹ לְאָכִישׁ, תְּנַאי הָיָה בֵּינֵינוּ (שמואל א יז:ט): ״אִם יוּכַל לְהִלָּחֵם אִתִּי וְהִכָּנִי וְהָיִינוּ לָכֶם לַעֲבָדִים״, אֵין לְךָ אֶלָּא שֶׁתֵּרֵד מִכִּסְאֲךָ, וְיִהְיֶה מֶלֶךְ הָאָרֶץ הַזֹּאת:


**Is this not Dovid, the king of the land.** They said to Achish, "There was a stipulation between us: 'If he will be able to fight me and kill me, then we will become your slaves.'¹⁰ You have no choice but to get off your throne and let him become king of this land."

footnotes:
¹⁰ Above 17:9.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:1)


**אֶת טַעְמוֹ.** אֶת דְּבָרָיו:


**His behavior.** His words.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:2)


**וַיִּתְהֹלֵל.** נִשְׁתַּטָּה:


**And feigned madness.** He acted insane.¹¹¹²

footnotes:
¹¹ The Midrash relates that Dovid said to ה׳ that he understood all creation of ה׳ with the exception of madness, for which he saw no purpose. ה׳ then responded that He will demonstrate to him the value of madness and that Dovid will need it.
¹² I.e., he wrote in a normal handwriting, irrational statements.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:3)


**וַיְתָו.** וַיִּכְתֹּב:


**He scribbled.** He wrote.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ He wrote, ‘Ochish the King of Gas owes me one hundred thousand gold coins and his wife owes me fifty.’


###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:14:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:14:4)


**רִירוֹ.** רוֹקוֹ, דָּבָר הַנּוֹטֵף קָרוּי רִיר, וְכֵן ״בְּרִיר חַלָּמוּת״ (איוב ו:ו); וְכֵן ״רָר בְּשָׂרוֹ״ (ויקרא טו:ג):


**His saliva.** His saliva; anything which drips is called 'ריר' as in, 'in saliva [בְּרִיר] of health',¹⁴ and as in, '[whether] his flesh run [דָר]'.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁴ Iyov 6:6.
¹⁵ Vayikra 15:3.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Samuel 21:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/21:16:1)


**הֲזֶה יָבֹא אֶל בֵּיתִי.** לְשׁוֹן תְּמִיהָה, וּלְכָךְ נָקוּד חֲטַף פַּתָּח, וְרָפֵי:


**Should such [a person] come into my house.** This is in the form of a question; it is [i.e., the 'ה'] therefore punctuated by a חֲטַף פַּתָּח and [the ז] is not punctuated by a דָגֵּשׁ.



## Chapter 22


### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:4:1)


**וַיֵּשְׁבוּ עִמּוֹ כָּל יְמֵי הֱיוֹת דָּוִד בַּמְּצוּדָה.** סָמוּךְ לְמוֹאָב, וּכְשֶׁיָּצָא דָוִד מִשָּׁם לְיַעַר חָרֶת, הָרַג מֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב אֶת אָבִיו וְאֶת אִמּוֹ וְאֶחָיו, חוּץ מֵאֶחָד שֶׁבָּהֶם שֶׁהֶחֱיָהוּ נָחָשׁ הָעַמּוֹנִי, וְהוּא הַחֶסֶד שֶׁאָמַר דָּוִד (שמואל ב י:ב): ״כַּאֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה אָבִיו עִמָּדִי חֶסֶד״, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּמִדְרַשׁ רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא:


**And they remained with him all the time that Dovid was in the fortress.** Near Moav, and when Dovid left from there to the forest of Cheres, the king of Moav killed his father, mother, and brothers, except for one of them, whom Nochosh the Ammonite raised.¹ This is the kindness which Dovid [referred to when he] said, "as his father did kindness with me,"² as it is stated in the Midrash of R. Tanchuma.

footnotes:
¹ Bamidbar Rabbah 14:1.
² II Shmuel 10:2.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:6:1)


**כִּי נוֹדַע דָּוִד.** כִּי הִתְבּוֹנֵן לְהִשָּׁמֵר מִמֶּנּוּ:


**That Dovid…had been discovered.** That he realized to beware of him.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:6:2)


**תַּחַת הָאֶשֶׁל בָּרָמָה.** אַחַת שֶׁהָיְתָה בִּגְבוּל בִּנְיָמִן וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (תענית ה ב): הִיא הָיְתָה רָמָה שֶׁל שְׁמוּאֵל, וּשְׁנֵי מְקוֹמוֹת הֵן, וְכֵן מִדְרָשׁוֹ: וְשָׁאוּל יוֹשֵׁב בַּגִּבְעָה, בִּזְכוּת הָאֶשֶׁל הַגָּדוֹל אֲשֶׁר בָּרָמָה, שֶׁהָיָה מִתְפַּלֵּל עָלָיו:


**Beneath the tamarisk tree in Romoh.** On one [hill], which was within Binyomin's border.³ Our Rabbis said⁴ that this was Shmuel's Romoh, and these are two different places. It is expounded thus: 'Shaul was sitting in Givoh in the merit of the great tamarisk tree [i.e., Shmuel] who was in Romoh, and who was praying for him.'⁵

footnotes:
³ Not to be confused with the Romoh where Shmuel lived.
⁴ Maseches Taanis 5b.
⁵ Were it not for Shmuel’s prayers, Shaul would have had to relinquish the throne immediately after the incident with Amoleik. Shmuel prayed for Shaul’s reign to remain intact as long as he was alive.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:13:1)


**וְשָׁאוֹל לוֹ בֵּאלֹהִים.** עֲשִׂיתוֹ מֶלֶךְ, שֶׁאֵין נִשְׁאָלִין בָּאוּרִים וְתֻמִּים לְהֶדְיוֹט:


**And inquiring of God for him.** [By allowing him to consult the Urim and Tumim], you have made him a king, because no inquiry may be made of the Urim and Tumim for a commoner.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:14:1)


**וְסָר אֶל מִשְׁמַעְתֶּךָ.** כְּמוֹ ״וַיָּסֻרוּ אֵלָיו״ (בראשית יט:ג), וְקָרַב לְשִׁמּוּשְׁךָ וְאֶל שְׁמַע דְּבָרֶיךָ, וְכִי הָיָה לִי לְהָבִין שֶׁאֵינוֹ הוֹלֵךְ בִּשְׁלִיחוּתְךָ:


**Who bows to your bidding.** As in "And they turned [וַיָסֻרוּ] to him,"⁶ meaning that he is near to service you and to obey your orders. How then was I to know that he was not going on your mission?

footnotes:
⁶ Bereishis 19:3.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:15:1)


**הַיּוֹם הַחִלֹּתִי לִשְׁאָל לוֹ.** בִּתְמִיהַּ, זֶה יָמִים רַבִּים שֶׁצֹּרֶךְ הַצִּבּוּר בּוֹ, שֶׁמִּנָּהוּ מוֹצִיא וּמֵבִיא אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל, וּמֵאָז אֲנִי שׁוֹאֵל לוֹ בֵּאלֹהִים:


**Did I begin today to inquire.** This is in the form of a question, meaning, it is already a long time that the concern of the community is his, for they appointed him as the one who leads [the Bnei] Yisroel to and [from battle], and ever since then, I inquire of God on his behalf.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:15:2)


**חָלִילָה לִּי.** מֵעֲשׂוֹת דָּבָר זֶה לִמְרֹד וּמַעַל:


**Far be it from me.** To have done this thing with the intent of rebellion or treason.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ I.e., I never would have acted against the king’s interests had I known that Dovid was fleeing.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:17:1)


**לָרָצִים הַנִּצָּבִים עָלָיו.** אַבְנֵר וַעֲמָשָׂא הָיוּ:


**To the runners who stood near him.** Referring to Avneir and Amoso.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:17:2)


**וְלֹא אָבוּ.** דָּרְשׁוּ אַכִּין וְרַקִּין (יהושע א:יח): כָּל אִישׁ אֲשֶׁר יַמְרֶה אֶת פִּיךָ וְגוֹ׳, יָכוֹל אֲפִלּוּ לִדְבַר עֲבֵרָה תַּלְמוּד לוֹמַר ׳רַק׳:


**Did not want.** They expounded [Scripture's] "but's and only's." Scripture states: "Every man who rebels against your command [shall be put to death]."⁸ One may think that this means even [if "your command" was] to commit a sin; Scripture, therefore states: "Only" [to exclude this condition].

footnotes:
⁸ Yehoshua  1:18.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:18:1)


**לְדוֹיֵג.** נִשְׁתַּנָּה שְׁמוֹ, אָמַר לוֹ נִתְפַּסְתָּ כְּדָג, אַתָּה הִלְשַׁנְתָּ עֲלֵיהֶם, אַתָּה הֲרֹג אוֹתָם:


**To Doeg.** [Doeg's] name changed [to Doyeig].⁹ He said to him, "You are caught like a fish;¹⁰ you informed on them so you slay them."

footnotes:
⁹ דַיָג means fisherman.
¹⁰ Based on Talmud Yerushalmi Maseches Sanhedrin 10:2. However, the Gemara in Maseches Sanhedrin 106b explains that דֹאֵג means worry, i.e., ה׳ was concerned that he might go astray. Once he did, ה׳ said, ‘וי’[= woe], it is lamentable that this brilliant person has gone astray, hence the דוֹיֵג spelling.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:22:1)


**אָנֹכִי סַבֹּתִי.** סִבַּבְתִּי הַדָּבָר עַד שֶׁבָּא לִידֵי כָּךְ:


**I am responsible for.** I brought about the matter, causing it to come.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Samuel 22:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/22:23:1)


**כִּי אֲשֶׁר יְבַקֵּשׁ אֶת נַפְשִׁי.** אָדָם אֶחָד רוֹדֵף שְׁנֵינוּ, בִּמְקוֹם שֶׁאִמָּלֵט שָׁם אֲנִי, תִּמָּלֵט אַתָּה, כִּי בְּמִשְׁמַרְתִּי תִּהְיֶה תָמִיד, כָּךְ תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״אֲרֵי מַאן דְּבָעֵי לְמִקְטְלִי בָּעֵי לְמִקְטְלָךְ״. וְיֵשׁ עוֹד לְפָרֵשׁ; כִּי אֲשֶׁר יֹאהֲבֵנִי יֹאהֲבְךָ, וַאֲשֶׁר יִשְׁמֹר אֶת נַפְשִׁי יִשְׁמֹר אֶת נַפְשֶׁךָ, וְיֵשׁ דֻּגְמָא בַּמִּקְרָא ״אַנְשֵׁי דָמִים יִשְׂנְאוּ תָם וִישָׁרִים יְבַקְשׁוּ נַפְשׁוֹ״ (משלי כט:י), הֲרֵי כָּאן בַּקָּשַׁת נֶפֶשׁ לְטוֹבָה:


**For the man who seeks my life.** One person is pursuing both of us. Wherever I escape, you escape, for you will always be in my safeguard; as Targum Yonoson renders, 'for he who seeks to kill me, seeks to kill you.' But, it may also mean, 'whoever will love me, will love you, and whoever will guard my soul, will guard your soul.' There is a similar instance in Scripture, "Men of blood hate a sincere person, but the upright seek his soul.'¹¹ This is [an instance of] seeking a soul for [doing] good.

footnotes:
¹¹ Mishlei 29:10.



## Chapter 23


### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:7:1)


**נִכַּר אֹתוֹ אֱלֹהִים בְּיָדִי.** הִסְגִּיר, וְכֵן (עובדיה א:יב): ״אַל תֵּרֶא בְיוֹם אָחִיךָ בְּיוֹם נָכְרוֹ״. וּמִמִּשְׁקַל הַחֲזָקִים הוּא, כְּמוֹ ״אִבַּד וְשִׁבַּר בְּרִיחֶיהָ״ (איכה ב:ט). וְאִם בִּקֵּשׁ לְדַבֵּר בְּמִשְׁקֹלֶת הָרָפִים, הָיָה נָקוּד נָכַר:


**s God has given him into my hand.** Meaning, delivered, and similarly, "And do not look on, on the day of your brother, on the day of his being delivered [נכרו]."¹ This is the conjugation of the strong [verbs e.g., Pi'el], as in, "He destroyed and broke her bars."² If he desired to speak in the conjugation of the weak [verbs e.g., Kal], it would have been punctuated thus: נָכַר.

footnotes:
¹ Ovadyoh 1:12.
² Eichah 2:9.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:7:2)


**נִסְגַּר לָבֹא בְּעִיר דְּלָתַיִם וּבְרִיחַ.** וְיִבְטַח בְּכָךְ, וְלֹא יָחוּשׁ לִבְרֹחַ מִמֶּנּוּ:


**For he has been shut in by entering a city that has doors and a bolt.** He will rely on that, and will not care to flee from it.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:9:1)


**מַחֲרִישׁ הָרָעָה.** (תרגום:) ״כְּמִין יָת בִּישְׁתָּא״:


**Planning evil.** Plotting the evil.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:11:1)


**הֲיַסְגִּרֻנִי בַעֲלֵי קְעִילָה וְגוֹ׳.** לָמַדְנוּ מִכָּאן, שֶׁהַשּׁוֹאֵל שְׁנֵי דְבָרִים, אֵין מְשִׁיבִין לוֹ אֶלָּא אֶחָד, וְאֵין מְשִׁיבִין לוֹ אֶלָּא רִאשׁוֹן, וְכָאן דָּוִד שָׁאַל שֶׁלֹּא כַּסֵּדֶר, שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ לִשְׁאוֹל תְּחִלָּה הֲיֵרֵד שָׁאוּל, הֲיַסְגִּירֻנִי בְיָדוֹ, וְהֵשִׁיבוּהוּ כַּסֵּדֶר:


**Will Keilah's residents hand me over…** This teaches us that one who consults regarding two matters, receives a reply concerning only one, and that reply concerns the first one. And here, Dovid asked in an inverted order,³ because he should have first asked,⁴ "Will Shaul come down? Will [the residents of Keilah] hand me over?" But he was answered according to the [proper] order.

footnotes:
³ Perhaps Dovid asked the questions in an inverted order because he was primarily interested whether Ke’ilah’s residents would hand him over to Shaul. He had assumed that Shaul would pursue him.
⁴ See Maseches Yoma 73a. Dovid should have asked first what were Shaul’s intentions and then whether Ke’ilah’s residents would hand him over to Shaul. He was answered on the question that he should have asked first that “he [Shaul] will go down.”



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:13:1)


**בַּאֲשֶׁר יִתְהַלָּכוּ.** (תרגום:) בַּאֲתַר דְּכָשַׁר לְהַלְּכָא״, בִּמְקוֹם הַצָּלָתָם״:


**Wherever they could go.** In a place where it is proper to go, in a place where they would be saved.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:16:1)


**וַיְחַזֵּק אֶת יָדוֹ.** חִדֵּשׁ בְּרִית שֶׁבֵּינֵיהֶם:


**And he strengthened his hand.** He renewed the covenant which was between them.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:20:1)


**וְלָנוּ הַסְגִּירוֹ.** וְעָלֵינוּ לְהַסְגִּירוֹ:


**And it is ours to hand him over.** It is our obligation to deliver him.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:22:1)


**כִּי אָמַר אֵלַי.** מִי שֶׁאָמַר:


**For [someone] told me.** The one who told.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ It can also be understood literally, that “he told me…” refers to Dovid. During the period that Dovid served before Shaul, he would describe to Shaul the cunning ways in which he outsmarted the Philistines.—Radak


###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:22:2)


**עָרוֹם יַעְרִם הוּא.** מִתְנַהֵג הוּא בְּעָרְמָה, הַיּוֹם כָּאן, וּלְמָחָר בְּמָקוֹם אַחֵר, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יִוָּדַע מְקוֹמוֹ:


**He is very cunning.** He is accustomed to behave cunningly, by being in one place today and tomorrow somewhere else, so that his place not be known.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:23:1)


**אֶל נָכוֹן.** עַל דָּבָר מְכֻוָּן וֶאֱמֶת:


**Well prepared.** With an accurate and true statement.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:24:1)


**לִפְנֵי שָׁאוּל.** שֶׁהָיָה שָׁאוּל עָתִיד לֵילֵךְ אַחֲרֵיהֶם, וְהֵם הָלְכוּ לְפָנָיו לְחַפֵּשׂ אֶל נָכוֹן:


**Ahead of Shaul.** For Shaul was destined to go after them, and they went before him, to search exactly [where Dovid was].



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:26:1)


**עֹטְרִים.** מַקִּיפִים וְסוֹבְבִים מִצַּד אֶל צַד, כַּעֲטָרָה הַמַּקֶּפֶת אֶת הָרֹאשׁ:


**Were surrounding.** Encircling and surrounding from side to side, like a crown which encircles the head.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:27:1)


**וּמַלְאָךְ בָּא אֶל שָׁאוּל.** מַלְאָךְ מַמָּשׁ, כְּדֵי לְהַצִּיל אֶת דָּוִד:


**A messenger came.** A real angel [in the guise of a messenger], in order to save Dovid.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Samuel 23:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/23:28:1)


**סֶלַע הַמַּחְלְקוֹת.** שֶׁהָיָה לִבּוֹ שֶׁל שָׁאוּל חָלוּק לִשְׁתֵּי דֵעוֹת, אִם לָשׁוּב לְהַצִּיל אֶת אַרְצוֹ מִיַּד פְּלִשְׁתִּים, אוֹ לִרְדֹּף וְלִתְפֹּשׂ אֶת דָּוִד, כֵּן תִּרְגְּמוֹ יוֹנָתָן:


**The "Rock of Divisions."** Shaul's heart was divided by two thoughts,⁶ whether to return to save his country from the Philistines, or to pursue and catch Dovid; thus renders Targum Yonoson.

footnotes:
⁶ Or it may refer to the division among Shaul’s troops as to whether to pursue Dovid or to battle the Philistines.—Radak



## Chapter 24


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:3:1)


**צוּרֵי הַיְעֵלִים.** סַלְעֵי הַיְּעֵלִים:


**The rocky land of the wild goats.** The rocks of the wild goats.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:4:1)


**לְהָסֵךְ אֶת רַגְלָיו.** לְהִפָּנוֹת לִנְקָבִים גְּדוֹלִים:


**To relieve himself.** To defecate.¹

footnotes:
¹ Maseches Yevamos 103a states that the term ‘לְהָסֵךְ אֶת דַגְלָיו’ is a euphamistic expression. Because of his extreme modesty, Shaul covered himself like a succah [Maseches Berachos 62b], with his clothing. The word לְהָסֵךְ originates from סִכוּדְ, which means covered.—Radak.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:6:1)


**וַיְהִי אַחֲרֵי כֵן וְגוֹ׳.**


**Afterwards … etc.**



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:7:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לַאֲנָשָׁיו חָלִילָה.** ״שְׁנֵי מִקְרָאוֹת הַלָּלוּ כְּתוּבִין שֶׁלֹּא כְּסִדְרָן, אֶלָּא כֵּיוָן שֶׁהִתְחִיל לְדַבֵּר בִּכְרִיתַת הַמְּעִיל, כִּלָּה כָּל דְּבָרָיו, וְאָמַר שֶׁאַף בִּכְרִיתַת הַמְּעִיל נִתְחָרֵט, וְאַחַר כָּךְ חוֹזֵר לְדִבְּרוֹ הָרִאשׁוֹן, וְאוֹמֵר; עַל שֶׁאָמְרוּ לוֹ אֲנָשָׁיו ״הִנֵּה הַיּוֹם וְגוֹ׳״, הֵשִׁיב לָהֶם, ״חָלִילָה לִי וְגוֹ׳:


**He said to his men, "[Adonoy] forbid."** These two verses are not written in sequence;² but since he began to speak regarding the cutting of the robe, he finished everything concerning it, and he said that he even regretted cutting the robe. Afterwards, he returns to the first subject, and says that regarding his men's comments, "Behold, concerning this day, etc." He replied to them, 'May Adonoy forbid that, etc.'

footnotes:
² According to Radak, these verses are written in chronological sequence, and this conversation took place after Dovid returned to his men with the cut piece of Shaul’s robe.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:8:1)


**וַיְשַׁסַּע.** הִבְדִּיל וְדָחָה אוֹתָם בִּדְבָרִים:


**Dovid pacified.** He separated and held them off with these words.³

footnotes:
³ The literal meaning of וַיְשַׁסַּע is ‘he divided or split’ as in Shoftim 14:6. By being tactful in speaking to his men, Dovid divided their unanimous opinion of wanting to attack Shaul.—Metzudos



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:10:1)


**לָמָּה תִשְׁמַע.** לָמָּה תְּקַבֵּל עָלַי לְשׁוֹן הָרַע שֶׁל דּוֹאֵג:


**Why do you listen.** Why do you accept Do'eg's slander concerning me?⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Rashi states that Dovid was alluding to Do’eg because Dovid said ‘why do you listen to the man [אָדָם]’, in the singular, referring to one individual person.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:11:1)


**וְאָמַר לַהָרְגָךְ וַתָּחָס עָלֶיךָ.** הֲרֵי כָּאן חֲסֵרִים שְׁתֵּי תֵבוֹת, וְאָמַר הָאוֹמֵר שֶׁאֶהֶרְגָךְ, וַתָּחָס נַפְשִׁי עָלֶיךָ, כְּמוֹ (שמואל ב יג:לט): ״וַתְּכַל דָּוִד לָצֵאת אֶל אַבְשָׁלוֹם״, וַתְּכַל נֶפֶשׁ דָּוִד:


**Someone said to kill you, but I had mercy on you.** There are two words missing here, "and one who spoke [הָאוֹמֵר] said that I should kill you, but my soul [נַפְשִׁי] had pity on you, similar to "and King Dovid longed to go forth to Avshalom,"⁵ [meaning,] and Dovid's soul [נֶפֶשׁ] longed.

footnotes:
⁵ II Shmuel 13:39.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:12:1)


**וְאָבִי רְאֵה.** כְּלוֹמַר תֵּן לִבְּךָ עַל הַדָּבָר, וְגַם רְאֵה הָעֵדוּת, אֲשֶׁר כְּנַף מְעִילְךָ בְּיָדִי, וְהָיִיתִי יָכוֹל לְהָרְגָךְ:


**My father, indeed see.** Meaning, pay attention to the matter, and also see the evidence, for the corner of your robe is in my hand, and I could have killed you.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ As easily as having cut off the corner of your robe.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:14:1)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר יֹאמַר מְשַׁל הַקַּדְמֹנִי.** מְשַׁל קַדְמוֹנוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם, הַתּוֹרָה, שֶׁהִיא מְשָׁלוֹ שֶׁל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא:


**As the ancient proverb says.** The proverb of the Ancient One of the world⁷ [referring to] the Torah, which is the proverb of the Holy One, Blessed is He.⁸

footnotes:
⁷ It may also mean ‘old proverb’ or it may refer to a ‘Kadmonite proverb,’ a nation mentioned in Bereishis 15:19.
⁸ And accordingly הַקַּדְמֹנִי means ‘The Ancient One’ referring to ה׳ Himself.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:14:2)


**מֵרְשָׁעִים יֵצֵא רֶשַׁע.** הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא מְזַמֵּן לָרָשָׁע, שֶׁנּוֹפֵל בְּיַד רָשָׁע כְּיוֹצֵא בּוֹ, וְהֵיכָן אָמְרָה תוֹרָה (שמות כא:יג): ״וְהָאֱלֹהִים אִנָּה לְיָדוֹ״ כִּדְאִיתָא בְּמַסֶּכֶת מַכּוֹת:


**From the wicked comes wickedness.⁹** The Holy One, Blessed is He, destines the wicked to fall into the hand of the wicked like himself. And where did the Torah say this, "And God destined it into his hand,"¹⁰ as it is stated in Maseches Makos.¹¹

footnotes:
⁹ So let it not come from me even if you deserve it.—Radak.
¹⁰ Shemos 21:13.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Samuel 24:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/24:21:1)


**יָדַעְתִּי כִּי מָלֹךְ תִּמְלֹךְ.** שֶׁרוֹאֶה אֲנִי שֶׁהַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא מַצִּילְךָ מִיָּדִי. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה(ילקוט שמעוני רמז קלד): סִימָן זֶה מָסַר לוֹ שְׁמוּאֵל, שֶׁהַקּוֹרֵעַ מְעִילוֹ, יִמְלֹךְ תַּחְתָּיו:


**I know that you will surely reign.** For I see that the Holy One, Blessed is He, saves you from my hand. The Midrash Aggadah states, that Shmuel gave him this sign, that the one who tears his robe will reign after him.¹²

footnotes:
¹² See above 15:27–28.



## Chapter 25


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:1)


**וְאִישׁ בְּמָעוֹן.** שֵׁם הָעִיר:


**There was a man in Ma'on.** The name of the city.¹

footnotes:
¹ Not to be mistook for מָעוֹן meaning a dwelling, as in Tehillim 26:8, but rather, it is the city mentioned in Yehoshua 15:55, and it was located in Yehudah’s territory, the tribe of which Novol was a member.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:2)


**וּמַעֲשֵׂהוּ.** מִקְנֵהוּ וְקִנְיָנוֹ:


**Whose business.** His cattle and possessions.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:3)


**גָּדוֹל.** עָשִׁיר:


**Wealthy.** [Literally 'very great'], meaning wealthy.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:2:4)


**וַיְהִי בִּגְזֹז אֶת צֹאנוֹ בַּכַּרְמֶל.** וַיְהִי יוֹם אֶחָד בְּהַגִּיעַ עֵת גְּזוֹז צֹאנוֹ בַּכַּרְמֶל, וְדַרְכּוֹ הָיָה לַעֲשׂוֹת מִשְׁתֶּה וְיוֹם טוֹב לְגוֹזְזֵי הַצֹּאן:


**It was the time when he was shearing his sheep in Carmel.** One day, when the time of his sheep shearing came around, he was in Carmel. His custom was to make a party and a festive day for the sheep shearers.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:3:1)


**וְהוּא כָלִבִּי.** מִבֵּית כָּלֵב:


**A Calebite.** Of the house of Caleiv.²

footnotes:
² Or, his evil nature was similar to that of a dog [= כֶּלֶב]; i.e., he was stingy and refused to share his food with others.—Ralbag



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:6:1)


**כֹּה לֶחָי.** כֹּה יִהְיֶה לְשָׁנָה הַבָּאָה, גּוֹזֵז (כֹּה לֶחָי. כֹּה יִהְיֶה לְשָׁנָה הַבָּאָה, מַזָּל חַי וְקַיָּם). (לָשוֹן אַחֵר כֹּה לֶחָי. אָמְרוּ עַל טִבְחוֹ וְנִסְכּוֹ שֶׁתִּרְאוּ, כֵּן רָאוּי לְאִישׁ חַי, לְאִישׁ זָרִיז וְחָשׁוּב, כְּמוֹ (שְׁמוּאֵל ב כג:כ): ״בֶּן אִישׁ חַי רַב פְּעָלִים״, חַי וּמְזֹרָז, בַּעַל מַעֲשִׂים, עַד כָּאן):


**So.** So will he be [shearing] in the coming year.³ So shall be in the coming year, living and enduring.) Another version is: **So to the living.** Say this concerning his meal and his wine that you will see, "So is befitting a live man, i.e., an active and esteemed man," as in "…was a live man of many heroic deeds,"⁴ [i.e.] alive and active in all his deeds.

footnotes:
³ See Rashi in Bereishis 18:10.
⁴ II Shmuel 23:20.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:6:2)


**לֶחָי.** טוֹב וְקַיָּם:


**May it always be so.** Good and enduring.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:6:3)


**וְאַתָּה שָׁלוֹם וְגוֹ׳.** כֵּן תִּהְיֶה לְאֹרֶךְ יָמִים:


**Peace unto you…** So shall it be for a long time.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ And to emphasize its longevity, they mention שַׁלוֹם [=peace] three times in their statement.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:7:1)


**לֹא הִכְלַמְנוּם.** אִם נִצְרְכוּ לָנוּ לְשׁוּם דָּבָר, לֹא הֱשִׁיבוֹנוּם רֵיקָם:


**We did not shame them.** If they needed us for anything, we did not send them back empty-handed.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ We did not cause them physical harm.—Targum Yonoson.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:7:2)


**וְלֹא נִפְקַד לָהֶם.** מְצָאנוּם כִּי שׁוֹמְרִים הָיִינוּ לָהֶם:


**Not a thing of theirs was missing.** Of your flocks, for we were watching them.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ And we protected them from attack by wild animals.—Mahri Kra.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:8:1)


**וְיִמְצְאוּ הַנְּעָרִים.** שֶׁלִּי, חֵן בְּעֵינֶיךָ, בַּטּוֹבָה שֶׁיַּגִּידוּ לְךָ עֲבָדֶיךָ מֵהֶם עֲלֵיהֶם:


**May the youths find.** My youths, find favor in your eyes, for the kindness which your servants will tell concerning them.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:8:2)


**כִּי עַל יוֹם טוֹב בָּאנוּ.** עַל סְמַךְ מִשְׁתֶּה וְיוֹם טוֹב שֶׁיֵּשׁ לְךָ, בָּאנוּ עַתָּה לִשְׁאוֹל. דָּבָר אַחֵר: עֶרֶב רֹאשׁ הַשָּׁנָה הָיָה, וּצְרִיכִין אָנוּ לִסְעוּדַת יוֹם טוֹב:


**For we have come on a festive day.** Relying on the party and festive day which you have, we have now come to ask.⁸ Another explanation is, that this was the eve of Rosh Hashonoh [so they said,] "We are in need of a holiday feast."⁹

footnotes:
⁸ Without burdening you additionally because you already have prepared a lavish feast.—Radak.
⁹ Rosh Hashanoh is a holiday in which Scripture [Nechemyah 8:10] stresses to send food to the needy.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:9:1)


**וַיְדַבְּרוּ אֶל נָבָל כְּכָל הַדְּבָרִים הָאֵלֶּה בְּשֵׁם דָּוִד וַיָּנוּחוּ.** יְגֵעִים הָיוּ מִטֹּרַח הַדֶּרֶךְ, וְנִזְדָּרְזוּ לְמִצְוַת דָּוִד, וְלֹא נָחוּ עַד שֶׁדִּבְּרוּ אֵלָיו, וְאַחַר כָּךְ וַיָּנוּחוּ. וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: ״וּפְסָקוּ״, כְּלוֹמַר פָּסְקוּ מִלְּדַבֵּר עוֹד, עַד שֶׁיִּשְׁמְעוּ תְּשׁוּבָתוֹ:


**And spoke to Novol in accordance with all these words, in Dovid's name, and then they rested.** They were tired from traveling, yet they were quick to execute Dovid's command, and did not rest until they spoke to him, and afterward they rested. Targum Yonson renders, however, and they ceased, i.e., they stopped speaking further, until they would hear his reply.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:10:1)


**מִי דָוִד.** מַה הוּא חָשׁוּב שֶׁאֶתֵּן לוֹ אֶת שֶׁלִּי, הֲלֹא מֵרוּת הַמּוֹאֲבִיָּה בָּא:


**Who is Dovid.** Of what importance is he that I should give him mine? Is he not descended from Rus the Moavite?¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ And as such, Novol questioned Dovid’s right to the throne. See Talmud Yerushalmi Sanhedrin 2:3.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:13:1)


**וַיַּחְגֹּר גַּם דָּוִד אֶת חַרְבּוֹ.** מִכָּאן שֶׁמַּתְחִילִין בְּדִינֵי נְפָשׁוֹת מִן הַקְּטַנִּים בְּדִין תְּחִלָּה (סנהדרין לו א):


**And Dovid too, girded his sword.** From here [we learn] that we commence, in cases involving capital punishment, from the less significant first.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ See Maseches Sanhedrin 36a, that learns from here that the polling the judges’ opinions in cases involving capital punishment, starts with the less important judges; otherwise, they may feel intimidated and swayed by the opinions of the others.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:13:2)


**עַל הַכֵּלִים.** לִשְׁמוֹר אָהֳלֵיהֶם וּכְלֵיהֶם:


**With the equipment.** To guard their tents and equipment.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:14:1)


**אֶחָד מֵהַנְּעָרִים.** שֶׁלָּהּ:


**One from among the youths.** Of hers.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:14:2)


**וַיָּעַט בָּהֶם.** (תרגום:) ״אַפְרְחִינוּן בְּמִלִּין״ (ילקוט שמעוני רמז קלד, וּפֵירוּשׁוֹ הִכְרִיחָם בִּדְבָרָיו הָרָעִים), לְשׁוֹן עַיִט (בראשית טו:יא):


**But he chased them away.** He drove them away with words, an expression of עַיׅט [=bird].¹²

footnotes:
¹² Bereishis 15:11. Others translate וַיָעַט as ‘he kicked them out’ from the root בעט, as in Devarim 32:15.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:15:1)


**וְלֹא הִכְלַמְנוּ.** לֹא נִכְלַמְנוּ:


**We were not shamed.** We were not disgraced.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:17:1)


**כִּי כָלְתָה הָרָעָה.** מֵאֵת דָּוִד אֵלָיו:


**For the evil has been decided.** By Dovid against him.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:17:2)


**וְהוּא בֶּן בְּלִיַּעַל מִדַּבֵּר אֵלָיו.** וַאֲדוֹנֵינוּ בֶּן בְּלִיַּעַל, מֵהֱיוֹת דּוֹבֵר אֵלָיו מַה שֶּׁאֲנִי אוֹמֵר אֵלֶיךָ, כִּי יִקְצֹף עָלֵינוּ:


**He is too wicked a person to speak to.** Our master is such a base person¹³ so that I cannot tell him that which I am telling you, because he will become angry with us.

footnotes:
¹³ Or, the phrase refers to Dovid, that Dovid is such a base person that he is capable of attacking without warning.—Radak.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:18:1)


**עֲשׂוּיוֹת.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״תַּכְבְּרָא״, מְמֻלָּאוֹת בָּשָׂר דַּק וּבֵיצִים, פשטי״ץ בלע״ז כְּמוֹ שֶׁשָּׁנִינוּ בִּפְסָחִים (עד א): וְנוֹתֵן אֶת הַכְּרָעַיִם וּבְנֵי מֵעָיו לְתוֹכוֹ, רַבִּי טַרְפוֹן קוֹרֵהוּ תַּכְבְּרָא, מַה שֶּׁבְּתוֹכוֹ לְבָרוֹ, כְּרַבִּי עֲקִיבָא דְּאָמַר תּוֹלִין חוּצָה לוֹ, כָּךְ הִיא שְׁנוּיָה בַּתּוֹסֶפְתָּא, רַבִּי קְלוֹנִימוֹס זצ״ל מִן רוֹמִי פֵּרְשָׁהּ לָנוּ כֵּן:


**Prepared.** Targum Yonoson renders תַּכְבְּרָא, filled with finely cut meat and eggs; farcides in O.F., as we learned in [Maseches] Pesachim:¹⁴ And he places the feet and its intestines inside [the Paschal lamb]; Rabbi Tarfon calls it תַּכְבְּרָא. That which is inside, is placed outside,¹⁵ according to Rabbi Akiva, who says that the intestines are hung outside of it. Thus is stated in the Tosefta, and Rabbi Klonimos of sainted memory, from Rome, thus explained it to us.

footnotes:
¹⁵ I.e., תַּכְבְּרָא is a combination of two words תּוֹךְ [=inside] and בְּרָא [=outside].


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:18:2)


**צִמּוּקִים.** עֲנָבִים יְבֵשִׁים:


**Raisins.** Dried grapes.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:18:3)


**דְּבֵלִים.** עִגּוּלֵי דְבֵלָה, תְּאֵנִים דְּרוּסוֹת וּכְבוּשׁוֹת בִּכְלִי:


**Cakes of pressed figs.** Round cakes of dried figs, trodden and pressed in a vessel.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:20:1)


**יוֹרְדִים לִקְרָאתָהּ.** גַּיְא הָיָה בֵּין שְׁנֵי הֶהָרִים, הִיא יוֹרֶדֶת מֵהַר זֶה, וְהֵם יוֹרְדִים מֵהַר זֶה שֶׁכְּנֶגְדּוֹ:


**Were going down toward her.** There was a valley between the two mountains. She was going down one mountain, and they were going down the opposite mountain.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ And neither party saw the other.—Radak.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:21:1)


**וְדָוִד אָמַר.** בְּלִבּוֹ כְּשֶׁאָמְרוּ לוֹ נְעָרָיו גִּדּוּפֵי נָבָל, **אַךְ לַשֶּׁקֶר שָׁמַרְתִּי וגו׳**:


**Dovid said.** To himself when his youths told him of Novol's insults, 'It was for naught that I guarded, etc.'



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:22:1)


**מַשְׁתִּין.** יוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: ״יָדַע מַדַּע״, מְשִׁית קִירוֹת לִבּוֹ. דָּבָר אַחֵר, אֲפִילוּ כֶּלֶב, שֶׁדַּרְכּוֹ לְהַשְׁתִּין בַּקִּיר:


**Even one dog.** Targum Yonoson renders, 'one possessing intelligence,' one who sets the walls of his heart. Another explanation is, 'even a dog whose manner is to urinate against a wall.'¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ See Maseches Bava Basro 19b.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:24:1)


**בִּי אֲנִי אֲדֹנִי הֶעָוֹן.** מִתְּחִלָּה אָמְרָה כָּךְ, כְּדֵי שֶׁיַּטֶּה אֹזֶן לִדְבָרֶיהָ, וּלְסוֹף אָמְרָה לוֹ הָאֱמֶת, אֲנִי אֲמָתְךָ לֹא רָאִיתִי אֶת נַעֲרֵי אֲדֹנִי:


**My master, with me lies the sin.** First she said this so that he would pay attention to her words.¹⁸ At the end, however, she told him the truth, that "I, your handmaid, did not see my master's youths."¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ And thereby protect her household from Dovid and his men.
¹⁹ Below, verse 25.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:26:1)


**אֲשֶׁר מְנָעָךְ.** שֶׁשְּׁלָחַנִי לִקְרָאתְךָ לְמָנְעֲךָ מִשְּׁפִיכוּת דָּמִים:


**[Adonoy] has prevented you.** Who sent me toward you to refrain you from committing bloodshed.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:26:2)


**וְהוֹשֵׁעַ יָדְךָ לָךְ.** מִלִּנְקוֹם אַתָּה בְּעַצְמְךָ אֶת נִקְמָתְךָ וְתֵעָנֵשׁ:


**And from avenging yourself by your own hand.** From personally avenging yourself and being punished.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:26:3)


**יִהְיוּ כְנָבָל אֹיְבֶיךָ.** נִתְנַבֵּאת בְּרוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ שֶׁלֹּא יַאֲרִיךְ יָמִים:


**May all your enemies be like Novol.** She prophesied with the Divine Spirit²⁰ that Novol will not live long.

footnotes:
²⁰ Avigayil is mentioned among the seven prophetesses, in Maseches Megillah 14a.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:27:1)


**וְנִתְּנָה לַנְּעָרִים.** וְתִנָּתֵן </b>לַנְּעָרִים הַמִּתְהַלְּכִים וְגוֹ׳<b>, ׳נִתְּנָה׳ לְשׁוֹן עָבַר, וְ׳נִתְּנָה׳ לְשׁוֹן לְהַבָּא:


**Let it be given to the youths.** And it will be given to the youths who are going, etc. נִתְּנָה [=it was given] is past tense, וְנִתְּנָה [=it will be given] is future tense.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:28:1)


**כִּי עָשֹׂה יַעֲשֶׂה ה׳ לַאדֹנִי בַּיִת נֶאֱמָן.** לִהְיוֹת מֶלֶךְ עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, לְפִיכָךְ, **וְרָעָה לֹא תִמָּצֵא בְךָ**, לְכָךְ לֹא נָאֶה לְךָ לַעֲשׂוֹת שֶׁלֹּא כַדִּין, לְהוֹצִיא לַעַז עַל מַלְכוּתְךָ:


**For Adonoy will surely make for my master an everlasting house.** To be king over Yisroel. Therefore, let no evil be found in you. It is therefore not fitting for you to do anything contrary to the law, to spread evil talk concerning your kingship.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:29:1)


**כַּף הַקֶּלַע.** חֲתִיכַת עוֹר רְחָבָה עֲשׂוּיָה כְּמִין כַּף בְּאֶמְצַע הַקֶּלַע, שֶׁנּוֹתֵן בָּהּ הָאֶבֶן:


**Slingshot.** A wide piece of leather, with an indentation in the middle of the sling, into which one places the stone.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:30:1)


**וְהָיָה כִּי יַעֲשֶׂה ה׳ וְגוֹ׳.** וְתִמְלוֹךְ:


**And may it be when Adonoy does good…** And you will reign.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:31:1)


**וְלֹא תִהְיֶה וְגוֹ׳ לְפוּקָה.** לְכִשָּׁלוֹן, כְּמוֹ (נחום ב:יא): ״לְפִיק בִּרְכַּיִם״, שֶׁאִם הָיִיתָ עוֹשֶׂה זֹאת, לֹא יִהְיֶה לְךָ פִּתְחוֹן פֶּה לִרְדּוֹת אָדָם לְדוֹרוֹת הַבָּאִים עַל שְׁפִיכוּת דָּמִים:


**That this not be…an obstacle.** As a stumbling block, as in, 'and the stumbling [לְפִיק] of the knees';²¹ for had you done this, you would not be able to open your mouth to reprove anyone in future generations, concerning bloodshed.

footnotes:
²¹ Nachum 2:11.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:31:2)


**וּלְהוֹשִׁיעַ אֲדֹנִי לוֹ.** שֶׁתִּנְקוֹם אַתָּה אֶת נִקְמָתְךָ:


**And that my master has avenged himself.** That you avenge yourself.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:33:1)


**כְּלִיתִנִי.** מְנַעְתְּ אוֹתִי, כְּמוֹ (תהלים מ:יב): ״לֹא תִכְלָא רַחֲמֶיךָ״; ״לֹא יִכְלֶה מִמְּךָ״ (בראשית כג:ו):


**Who prevented me.** You stopped me, as in, 'You shall not stop [תִכְלָא] your mercy,'²² [and] 'will not stop [יׅכְלֶה] you.'²³

footnotes:
²² Tehillim 40:12.
²³ Bereishis 23:6.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:33:2)


**וְהוֹשֵׁעַ יָדִי לִי.** מוּסָב עַל **מִבּוֹא בְדָמִים**, מ״ם שֶׁל מִבּוֹא מְחוּבֶּרֶת עַל וְהוֹשֵׁעַ, כְּמוֹ מִבּוֹא בְדָמִים וּמֵהוֹשֵׁעַ יָדִי לִי:


**And from avenging myself with my own hand.** This is connected with [the phrase], 'from coming into bloodshed.' The 'מ' [=from] of [מִבּוֹא] is connected to 'and avenging,' meaning, 'from coming into bloodshed and from avenging myself with my own hand.'



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:37:1)


**בְּצֵאת הַיַּיִן מִנָּבָל.** (תרגום:) ״כַּד פַּג חַמְרָא מִנָּבָל״:


**When Novol had sobered up.** When the wine's effect had left Novol.


###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:37:2)


**וַיָּמָת לִבּוֹ.** שֶׁנִּצְטַעֵר עַל הַמִּנְחָה שֶׁהוּבְאָת לְדָוִד:


**And his heart sank within him.** He was extremely upset over the gift presented to Dovid.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ I.e., because of the great expense of his wife’s gift.—Radak



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on I Samuel 25:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20I%20Samuel/r/25:38:1)
